# Page Revamp Gate

> ⚠️ **RETIRED AS THE OPERATING FRAME — 2026-07-07.** The *page-by-page visual admission* method below is superseded. There is now **one canonical guide + goal**, in two living docs: the **design-system canon** for visual — [`CONSOLE_DESIGN_SYSTEM_FROM_APP.md`](../design-system/CONSOLE_DESIGN_SYSTEM_FROM_APP.md) + [`AGENT_HANDSHAKE`](../../../tools/automation/AGENT_HANDSHAKE.md) + [`MANAGEMENT_PAGE_STANDARDS` v2.0](../ui-ux/MANAGEMENT_PAGE_STANDARDS.md) — and the **data-sync remediation** for backend authority — [`DATA_SYNC_REMEDIATION_AUDIT.md`](../database/DATA_SYNC_REMEDIATION_AUDIT.md). This gate's **spine carried forward, not discarded**: it always said the real gate was *backend authority + app-consequence proof against `ivisit-app`*, which the data-sync remediation now itemizes. **Still active — NOT historical:** (1) fail-closed / no-parallel-truth / app-consequence discipline, (2) the UI hardgate + contract-test preservation locks, (3) the rendered-proof bar before "done", and (4) **every Git-safety rule below** (never push `main`; checkpoint-branch only; preservation baseline `f31f29f`). Kept for history + the per-page ledgers; new work follows the two canon docs above, not this frame.

This is the operating rule for the console revamp:

`One page at a time. RBAC first. Layout relationship second. Visual design third. Engineering last.`

Do not start the next page until the current page proves the full gate below.

## Preservation Baseline Re-Anchor - 2026-07-07

This note is loud on purpose: the checkpoint commits advanced `HEAD` past the preservation baseline `f31f29f`, so any evidence phrased as `git show HEAD:<old page>` no longer reads the old page.

- The console revamp was uncommitted worktree source on top of `f31f29f` when the earlier ledgers were written, so at that time `HEAD` and the preservation baseline were the same commit. That is why older notes say the baseline "remains committed `HEAD` at `f31f29f`."
- The safety checkpoint then committed the revamp: `15acf6c checkpoint: preserve console revamp gate work` and `988ccf9 docs: record checkpoint branch push decision`. `HEAD` is now `988ccf9`, and `git show HEAD:<page>` returns the revamped file, not the old one.
- The preservation baseline is now the explicit commit `f31f29f` (the last pre-revamp committed sprint pack). So every `git show HEAD:<old page>` reference in this gate and earlier ledgers means the preservation baseline commit `f31f29f` now that `HEAD` has advanced. Read old behavior with `git show f31f29f:<old page>`.
- The contract-test preservation helpers are pinned to `f31f29f`: `PageRevampGate.contract.test.js`, `TodayHome.contract.test.js`, and every page intake/admission contract test now resolve their `gitShowHead`/`headSource` old-behavior reads through the `f31f29f` baseline instead of the moving `HEAD` ref, so preservation proofs stay honest after the checkpoint.
- No page admission status changed from this re-anchor. It is a proof-mechanism repair only: the default UI hardgate, the strict-radius revamp hardgate for the Today/Requests pair, and the full contract suite pass against the now-committed source.
- Future rule: if the preservation baseline is ever re-pinned to a newer pre-change commit, update this note and the contract-test baseline constant together, and never let a preservation proof read the moving `HEAD` ref again.

## Desktop Data-Render Canon - 2026-07-08

This globalizes the Requests-scoped "Active Requests handled-sheet lock" (below) into a
cross-page canon and records it as an explicit user decision. The readable home is
`MANAGEMENT_PAGE_STANDARDS.md` §1.5; this entry is the contract-locked statement.

- Desktop management pages render records exactly one way: ONE row/table projection inside the
  handled sheet (`RequestRow` in the reference). There is no `ViewToggle`, no `useViewMode`, and
  no grid/card/list mode switching on desktop.
- Card / grid / list view files (`XListView`, `*GridView`, card-view variants) are legacy-inactive:
  kept in the tree as the preservation anchor, never imported by the active route. Reintroduction
  is allowed only as a variant of the same route-owned projection after a page ledger records it —
  never as a second source of truth (see §Requests-As-Multi-Data line "not as separate truth").
- Why (grounded, not invented): one render proves "no second source of truth" once; the KPI/state
  chips already own signal survey; the DetailRail already owns per-record focus; one projection is
  one `service → hook → row → payload` contract to hardgate; reuse the canon, not the markup.
- Scope is desktop only. Mobile keeps its recomposed card/row rhythm (`MobileX`, a separate design
  system in `MOBILE_DESIGN_SYSTEM.md`) — that is not a desktop toggle.
- Roll-out is per-page and gated, not a repo-wide rip: `ViewToggle`/`useViewMode` still remain on
  Ambulances, Doctors, HealthNews, Hospitals, Insurance, Organizations, Pricing, Subscription,
  Users, Verification, and Visits. Each page converts the canon way — old-behavior ledger, mark the
  card/list variants `legacy inactive` (a preservation decision, not a silent drop), drop the toggle
  from the active route, keep the one row/table render, contract test stays green.
- The reference `EmergencyRequestsPage.jsx` already ships this: zero `ViewToggle`, one `RequestRow`
  render. The gate contract test locks both this statement and that behavioral fact.

These require shared backend truth and a browser proof session, not console-source edits.
- Goal status: the console revamp is NOT complete. The source-closable safety layer is essentially done across all pages, but the remaining work is per-page backend-authority proof, projection-owner design, action-model decisions, strict-radius visual admission, and rendered proof. Do not mark the goal complete until each admitted page has current triad plus rendered proof and each intake page is either admitted through the gate or explicitly kept out of scope with its backend blocker named.

## Rendered Proof Access Guide - 2026-07-07

This guide exists so future Claude Code sessions do not stop at "rendered proof is needed."

- Local rendered proof should use `frontend/.env.local`. This file is intentionally ignored by Git and must remain uncommitted.
- `frontend/.env.local` contains the Supabase runtime variables used by the local console (`REACT_APP_SUPABASE_URL`, `REACT_APP_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`, and related local keys) plus local proof credentials.
- The local proof credential keys are `IVISIT_TEST_ADMIN_EMAIL` and `IVISIT_TEST_ADMIN_PASSWORD`. Do not print their values in logs, docs, commits, screenshots, or final answers.
- If a page requires authentication, start or reuse the local frontend server from `frontend`, open `/login`, sign in with the local proof credential keys, and then navigate to the target route. Do not create a profile, promote a role, or mutate auth state to make proof pass.
- If a page is public (`/login`, `/set-password`, `/onboarding`, `/onboarding-success`, `/unauthorized`, or the `*` catch-all), render it directly when possible. Only sign in when the route's gate explicitly needs authenticated redirect or denial behavior.
- Reuse an existing `localhost:3000` server when it is already running. If a new server is required, start one cleanly, record the port, and stop it when proof is complete. Do not leave many local dev servers running.
- Browser proof must capture desktop and mobile where the gate asks for rendered proof, plus the target interaction state. Save screenshots outside the repo, report their paths, and verify no framework overlay, no horizontal overflow, no duplicate nav, no unintended authenticated-shell leakage, and no relevant console warnings/errors.
- Rendered proof does not bypass backend authority. If a button still lacks receiver/RLS/RPC/app-consequence proof, keep it unavailable or documented as intake-only even if it renders cleanly.

## Canonical Audit Reset - 2026-06-25

Before expanding the revamp again, treat Today plus Requests as the canonical layout audit pair.

The operating sequence for every page is now:

`audit old behavior -> preserve function/data -> revamp UI -> confirm canonical -> reuse globally`

The old Git-backed behavior must be represented before a new UI is accepted. If a previous table, list, card, modal, filter, action, realtime subscription, pagination path, or responsive behavior is not kept, the page ledger must say whether it was preserved, converted into the new pattern, excluded for lack of receiver/app consequence, or left as a legacy inactive surface.

### Active Goal Update - 2026-06-25

The active revamp goal is resumed as a canonical audit gate, not as a broad page-build sprint.

Current outputs required before wider expansion:

- A confirmed app shell contract for the left rail/sidebar, top navbar, footer, right context panel, notification center, FABs, modals, drawers, dropdowns, and responsive states.

- A reusable Requests pattern for multi-data rendering: signal field, state choices, handled sheet, focused detail rail, route-owned action, immediate feedback, and mobile recomposition.
- A design-system canon aligned with the iVisit app direction: simple language, one primary action, progressive reveal, quiet hierarchy, local non-danger semantic colors, and visible tap/hover/pending feedback.

The next page is not admitted because it visually resembles Requests. It is admitted only after it passes the next page admission gate below. The current likely continuation is Approvals at `/verification`, but it must restate old behavior, RBAC, shell relationship, source owner, unavailable actions, responsive owner, and Today/Requests fit before new visual or functional changes.

### Active Goal Resume - 2026-07-04

The goal is resumed as a canon-confirmation pass before any further page expansion. The current work is not a generic redesign sprint and does not authorize a new page to copy Requests. It confirms the operating system for the console: shared shell first, page relationship second, old Git preservation third, Requests pattern extraction fourth, and visual implementation last.

This resume pass uses the following source audit:

- Layout architecture: `App.js`, `LayoutContext.jsx`, `SmartHeader.jsx`, `IslandNavigation.jsx`, `ConsoleModuleRail.jsx`, `DynamicBottomBar.jsx`, `ContextAwareFAB.jsx`, `ContextPanelShell`, `NotificationCenter`, `FilterSheet`, and `ModalShell.jsx`.
- Page relationship: `TodayHome.jsx` and `EmergencyRequestsPage.jsx` both use `usePageShell({ bleed: true, hideFab: true })`, hide the shared footer, use shell-owned headers, and render the shared module rail instead of private page sidebars.
- Visible shell navigation groups are `Care`, `Admin`, `Payments`, and `Account`. `Operations`, `Management`, and `Finance` are not user-facing group labels in the active canon.
- Mobile navigation: the top-left avatar/account button opens the overflow sheet; the bottom island owns role-primary navigation. The avatar/account sheet may contain `Menu` and `Quick Actions` tabs, but it is not a second bottom-bar menu or hamburger entry point.
- The avatar/account sheet auto-expands the current visible group for Care, Admin, and Payments routes. It must not leave the active route hidden inside a collapsed group on mobile.
- Old Git preservation: `git show HEAD:frontend/src/components/pages/BentoHome.jsx` and `git show HEAD:frontend/src/components/pages/EmergencyRequestsPage.jsx` remain the preservation anchors for Today and Requests at checkpoint commit `f31f29f`.
- Requests canon: `getEmergencyRequestsPage()` and `getEmergencyRequestsPageStats()` remain the route read/stat owners; old list/table/card density, modal, filter, side-effect, action, subscription, pagination, loading, empty, error, and mobile behaviors are represented before conversion or exclusion.
- Design-system canon: one clear primary action per moment, simple UI-facing English, progressive reveal, structural loading/error/empty states, local non-danger semantic colors, immediate press/opening/pending feedback, shared `ModalShell`, shared `FilterSheet`, no private dropdown style, and no red unless danger, emergency, failed telemetry, or destructive meaning is real.
- Global geometry canon: the new console UI uses iVisit squircles. `src/index.css` owns the `geo-*` legacy aliases, legacy size aliases such as `squircle-xl` / `squircle-sm`, and semantic squircle utilities, all mapped to radius tokens instead of zero-radius sharp corners. `tailwind.config.js` exposes `rounded-sheet`, `rounded-card`, `rounded-inner`, `rounded-icon`, `rounded-button`, `rounded-pill`, `rounded-modal`, and `rounded-squircle`. The default UI hardgate checks `App.js`, `src/index.css`, and `tailwind.config.js` for shared shell/global geometry proof, sharp global geometry, hardcoded global CSS radii, and nonzero global CSS letter spacing. Candidate revamp files must also pass `npm run check:ui-hardgate:revamp -- <files>` before admission so later pages cannot claim the canon while reintroducing generic Tailwind radius utilities, arbitrary `rounded-[...]` values, legacy `geo-*` shape utilities, or size-named `squircle-*` shortcuts.
- Global color bridge: console runtime brand red is owned by `--primary: 357 74% 26%`; Tailwind's `brand` alias must resolve through `hsl(var(--primary))`, not a parallel `--color-brand` fallback. App-derived design examples may say `--color-brand`, but console implementation must map the intent back to the single global theme token.

Checkpoint decision: Today and Requests stay the only canonical reference pair. The next page may continue only after its own ledger proves old behavior preservation, RBAC/action authority, data ownership, page-to-shell relationship, responsive recomposition, interaction feedback, hardgate coverage, rendered desktop/mobile proof, and local process cleanup.

### User Canon Checkpoint - 2026-07-04

This checkpoint records the current product decision before the revamp expands again:

- Today is the role-first home. It does not carry the old dashboard's full multi-data burden.
- Requests is the page that absorbs the old multi-data work: list/table/card density, filters, modals, realtime refresh, pagination, mobile recomposition, detail focus, and action feedback.
- Requests becomes the reusable multi-data reference only after the old Git-backed behavior is represented and each old behavior is classified as `preserved`, `converted`, `excluded until receiver proof`, or `legacy inactive`.
- The shared shell is the product frame: left sidebar/rail, top navbar, footer visibility, right context panel, notification dropdown, FABs, modals, drawers, dropdowns, filters, and responsive states belong to shared shell/components.
- A page lives inside that shell. It must not add private sidebar, header, modal chrome, filter language, dropdown style, footer, FAB model, notification dropdown, or data-rendering behavior unless the page ledger records an approved canonical exception.
- The canonical user-facing style remains simple English, one clear primary action, progressive reveal, quiet hierarchy, structural loading/error/empty states, and immediate hover, tap, opening, pending, success, failure, or disabled feedback.
- This is the gate for every page: audit old behavior, preserve function and data, revamp UI, confirm the page-specific canon, then reuse globally.

### Canonical App Shell

The shared shell owns app chrome. Pages consume the shell; pages do not invent shell.

- `App.js` owns the authenticated app frame, `main-content`, scroll container, bleed mode, `SmartHeader`, `ResponsiveSidebar`/context panel shell, `SmartFooter`, desktop FAB mounting, and `DynamicBottomBar`.
- `LayoutContext.jsx` owns sidebar width/mode, route header content, route footer content, context-panel open state, and `pageShellConfig`.
- `usePageShell({ bleed, hideFab })` is the page contract. Full-screen stage pages use `bleed: true`; route-owned action pages use `hideFab: true`.
- `SmartHeader` owns route title, top search affordance, notification trigger, context-panel trigger, filter/view controls supplied by the page, and mobile account/menu trigger.
- Desktop module navigation is `ConsoleModuleRail`; it is role-filtered through `consoleModuleRail.js`. A page may mark the active path, but it must not create a second sidebar.
- Mobile navigation is `DynamicBottomBar` plus the top-left avatar/account sheet. The bottom island holds role-primary destinations only. There is no separate hamburger button unless a future canonical shell decision replaces this pattern.
- `ContextPanelShell` is for read/context/detail support. It closes on route changes and modal-open events. A page-level right rail may exist only when the page owns the focused workflow, as Requests does.
- `SmartFooter` is hidden on full-bleed operational pages unless the page proves footer content is primary to the workflow.
- Global FABs are off on route-owned pages. If a route has one clear action, the page or route-owned mobile action owns it.
- `NotificationCenter` belongs to the shell and must stay quiet/degradable. Notification reads cannot be counted as page domain truth.
- `ModalShell` is the modal chrome standard: app-owned backdrop, ARIA dialog semantics, scroll containment, and suppression of header/bottom-bar chrome on mobile. New modals should use it or document why they cannot yet.

### Shell Architecture Source Recertification - 2026-07-04

Current source recertifies the shared shell as the owner of console chrome:

- `App.js` mounts one authenticated frame: `SmartHeader`, desktop `IslandNavigation`, `main-content`, `ContextPanelShell`, `SmartFooter`, `ContextAwareFAB`, `DynamicBottomBar`, startup overlay, and global financial modals.
- `LayoutContext.jsx` owns route shell state: header content, footer content, sidebar width/mode, context-panel open state, and `pageShellConfig` with `bleed` and `hideFab`. `usePageShell()` resets the shell back to non-bleed/non-hidden-FAB on unmount.
- `SmartHeader.jsx` owns top title, shared search, notification trigger, context-panel trigger, and the top-left mobile account sheet. The account sheet remains the mobile overflow owner; there is no separate hamburger.
- Shared header fallback labels must match the role/nav canon: `/` is `Home` only as a browser-back fallback, `/emergencies` is `Requests`, `/doctors` is `Staff`, `/verification` is `Approvals`, and `/wallet` is `Payments`. A page cannot keep older developer or database names in shell chrome after the route/nav contract has been renamed.
- The desktop right-panel trigger is user-facing `Quick Actions`: it has a hover tooltip, `aria-expanded`, `aria-controls="quick-actions-panel"`, and `data-state="open|closed"`. Mobile keeps the same idea inside the avatar/account sheet as the `Quick Actions` tab; `Context` is not user-facing navigation copy.
- `IslandNavigation.jsx` owns desktop primary navigation and sidebar layout settings. `ConsoleModuleRail.jsx` owns page-local module rails for Today/Requests-style stages; pages may pass active path, not create a new app sidebar.
- `DynamicBottomBar.jsx` owns the mobile bottom island and route-owned mobile actions. It suppresses the generic context FAB when `pageShellConfig.hideFab` is true or the route owns its action.
- `ContextAwareFAB.jsx` is hidden on mobile, when the right context panel is open, and on route-owned action pages.
- `ContextPanelShell` is desktop-only, closes on Escape, closes on route change through `LayoutContext`, and listens for explicit `closeContextPanel`/modal-open events.
- `NotificationCenter` is shell chrome with quiet cached fetches, stale-response protection, realtime refresh, optimistic read feedback, and no page-domain truth authority.
- `QuickSearch` is the shared search dialog. It uses hidden dialog title/description, clear search feedback, hardgated chrome, and the current `Requests` category label for `/emergencies` results instead of the old `Emergency Requests` copy.
- `FilterSheet` is the shared filter drawer/sheet for route filters; it owns ARIA dialog semantics, mobile bottom-island suppression, route-specific clear/reset, and neutral ordinary action color.
- `ModalShell.jsx` is now in the default UI surface hardgate. It exposes `data-modal-shell="true"`, suppresses modal chrome and `#dynamic-bottom-bar`, uses one ARIA dialog surface, wraps subtitle copy on mobile, and separates optional footers with soft surface depth instead of border/hairline chrome.

Shell data side-effect cleanup, 2026-07-05:

- `useSupportTickets()` now has explicit `autoFetch`, `autoSubscribe`, and `quiet` options. The default preserves legacy hook behavior for a future direct page consumer.
- `ContextAwareFAB.jsx` and `DynamicBottomBar.jsx` call `useSupportTickets({ autoFetch: false, autoSubscribe: false, quiet: true })`, so global create-ticket affordances can submit through the modal without mounting Support list reads or realtime subscriptions on unrelated routes.
- `authService.getCurrentUser()` suppresses only the known Supabase auth-lock abort (`AbortError: signal is aborted without reason`) at the service boundary. Other current-user/profile errors remain visible unless a caller explicitly passes `quiet`.
- Reuse rule: global shell chrome may expose command affordances, but it may not acquire page-domain truth, realtime channels, or broad support/insurance/analytics datasets outside the page that owns that route.

Rendered shell/modal proof, 2026-07-04:

- Reused the existing local `3000` dev server and signed in with the admin account only after `/emergencies?proof=shell-modal-canon-desktop` redirected to login.
- Desktop `1440x900` opened Requests, then `New request`, and rendered one shared `data-modal-shell="true"` / `role="dialog"` / `aria-modal="true"` modal with `aria-labelledby`, no horizontal overflow, no desktop bottom bar, and no private page modal chrome.
- Mobile `390x844` opened Requests with the top-left account/Home model and bottom island, then opened the route-owned `New request` action. The shared modal rendered one ARIA dialog, exposed a `Close` control, hid `#dynamic-bottom-bar` with `aria-hidden="true"` / invisible / non-interactive state, kept horizontal overflow at `0`, and avoided clipped mobile subtitle copy.
- Browser console output for this proof showed only React DevTools info, PWA banner info, and one login autocomplete hint. No Requests/modal runtime warning or error was produced.

Reuse decision: a page may consume these shell pieces, but it may not add private app chrome, private modal wrappers, private notification dropdowns, private right-panel shells, private mobile menu buttons, or private filter/dialog surfaces unless the page ledger records a product-approved canonical exception.

### Canonical Interaction Surface Ownership - 2026-07-04

These surfaces are part of the design system, not page decoration. A page can configure them, but it cannot quietly invent a new version.

| Surface | Canonical owner | Required behavior |
|---|---|---|
| Modal trigger | Route action, shared header control, route-owned mobile action, or context-panel action that the page handles. | The trigger must show immediate pressed/opening/disabled feedback and name the receiver or unavailable reason before the modal opens. No global/private modal mount may bypass the page ledger. |
| Modal design | `ModalShell` plus the domain modal body. | One ARIA dialog, shell chrome suppressed on mobile, scroll contained in the modal body, simple title/subtitle/action copy, and one primary submit action. |
| Drawer/sheet | `FilterSheet`, `ModalShell`, or an approved shell drawer. | Drawers/sheets must close on Escape/backdrop/route change where appropriate, avoid bottom-island overlap, and use the same neutral ordinary-action color model. |
| Dropdown/menu | Shared shell or common UI dropdown primitives. | Dropdowns are for secondary choices only; they need focus/hover feedback, keyboard reachability, and no private visual language or page-specific menu chrome. |
| Filters | Shared `FilterSheet` plus route-owned filter state. | Filter changes map to the route service/query owner, reset pagination, show active-filter feedback, and separate empty results from failed loads. |
| Tabs/segmented controls | Shared tab/view primitives when a page ledger keeps density variants. | Tabs must switch variants of the same projection, not new data truth; selected state must be visible and mobile must reduce choices if density crowds the first screen. |
| Cards, rows, and tables | Page scan surface using the route projection. | Cards, rows, and tables are alternate renderers of one normalized projection. They cannot own separate fetches, fake counts, local-only complete truth, or hidden hover-only primary actions. |
| Empty/loading/error states | Page body using shared structural state patterns. | Loading keeps the final layout shape, empty states point to the next useful action, and errors show retry/unavailable truth instead of clear or zero. |
| Right panel/detail rail | `ContextPanelShell` for shell context or an approved page-owned focused rail. | Read/context detail lives here; write work moves to modal/receiver-backed action. Secondary actions stay hidden or unavailable until authority is proved. |
| Notifications | `NotificationCenter` in the shell. | Notifications may acknowledge system/user events, but notification reads cannot become page-domain truth or page-specific dropdowns. |
| Responsive behavior | Shared shell plus page-specific recomposition declared in the ledger. | Desktop space may become context; mobile must simplify into signal, compact choices, handled sheet, row reveal, and the next useful action. No private hamburger unless the shell canon changes. |

### Page-To-Layout Relationship

Every page must declare its relationship to the shell before visual work:

- Is the page normal content, full-bleed stage, canvas, table workspace, or modal-led workflow?
- Which route owns page data, and which global providers must stay asleep on that route?
- Which shell controls are visible: header, left rail/sidebar, right context panel, footer, desktop FAB, mobile bottom island, mobile route FAB?
- Which element owns vertical scroll: `main-content`, a page sheet, a table body, a modal body, or a mobile bottom sheet?
- Which page regions are allowed: signal field, KPI choice strip, activity sheet, detail rail, list, table, card grid, modal, drawer, empty/loading/error state.
- Which visible action is primary at first glance, and which secondary actions are progressively revealed?
- Which responsive recomposition is canonical: desktop to mobile must not merely shrink; it must reduce choices and preserve the first meaningful action.

No page may add a private sidebar, private header, private footer, private notification dropdown, private modal chrome, private FAB model, private filter language, or private dropdown style unless the page ledger records a product-approved canonical exception.

### Requests As Multi-Data Canonical

Requests is the canonical reference for pages that render multiple live data slices.

Current canonical Requests pattern:

- Route copy is `Requests`; canonical path remains `/emergencies`.
- Historical proof notes below may quote old titles such as `Emergency Requests | iVisit Console` or old headings such as `Emergency Logs`. Those notes are retained as evidence of earlier behavior, but they are superseded by the current `Requests` canon and must not be used as present-day UI acceptance copy.
- Route/nav permission resource is `emergency_requests`, matching the Supabase table and Requests service owner. The old `emergencies` label remains a path/id compatibility alias only, not a separate RBAC truth.
- Route data owner is `getEmergencyRequestsPage()` in `emergencyService`, with exact count, role scope, filters, pagination, payment enrichment, row normalization, and degraded states.
- Route stat owner is `getEmergencyRequestsPageStats()` through the same Requests service path. Active Requests no longer reads `PageDataContext` emergency stats for KPI, signal, mobile, or analytics rendering while `/emergencies` is route-owned.
- Desktop is a full-bleed stage: atlas background, left module rail, large signal field, compact KPI choices, handled activity sheet, and one focused right detail rail.
- Mobile is a recomposed stage: avatar/account sheet, bottom island, route-owned action when allowed, signal first, 2x2 state choices, handled bottom sheet, compact search/filter controls, row reveal, and one contextual action.
- The signal field owns first-glance truth. It must not use danger styling for clear/non-danger states.
- KPI choices filter the same canonical query shape. Local-only status choices are allowed only when the service still owns the source list/count truth.
- UI filter state is not backend query state until the route service normalizes it. Empty arrays, blank strings, empty date objects, nulls, and cleared filter defaults must mean no constraint unless the page ledger documents a different receiver contract.
- The activity sheet owns scanning. It may render cards, rows, or a table only as variants of the same data projection, not as separate truth.
- The detail rail owns the current focused item and one primary action. Secondary actions reveal below the primary action only when the receiver is proved.
- Create request uses shared `EmergencyRequestModal` on `ModalShell` with simple `New request` / `Create request` language, responsive status labels, open-only requester profile lookup, and the existing receiver-backed create/update payload. It is the route-owned action path when role allows it.
- Full request details use shared `EmergencyDetailsModal` on `ModalShell`; request evidence, cash approval, retry bridge, and visit bridge stay receiver-owned instead of becoming page chrome.
- Payment retry remains a proved receiver-backed secondary action, but the saved-method chooser now uses shared `ModalShell` chrome instead of private page-owned backdrop/dialog markup.
- Empty, loading, and error states must match final layout structure: no blank pauses, no fake clear counts, no complete truth from failed/capped reads.

Legacy Git behavior represented for Requests:

- Old committed Requests had grid/list/table view modes, KPI cards, filter sheet, analytics modal, pagination, realtime `emergency_requests` and `payments` subscriptions, create modal, details modal, confirmation modal, cash settlement modal, payment retry modal, dispatch, complete, cancel, retry, and mobile pull-to-refresh/infinite-load behavior.
- The new active Requests page preserves the route, service-backed read, exact count, filters/search/status filtering, pagination, realtime refresh, create/details/filter/analytics/confirmation/retry modal paths, dispatch/complete/cancel/retry receivers, mobile pull-to-refresh, infinite load, loading, empty, and route-owned mobile action behavior.
- The old grid/list/table switching is not canonical in the active Requests stage. The old list/table files remain legacy inactive surfaces; if reintroduced, they must be audited against the same service owner, receiver ownership, hardgate, and responsive rules before they count as shipped UI.
- Cash settlement remains excluded from ordinary page action shipping until the finance receiver/app consequence is proved. A visible cash follow-up must stay unavailable or explicitly blocked.
- Bulk cancel/select remains excluded from the active Requests stage until destructive receiver authority, role scope, confirmation, and app consequence are proved.
