Requests native dialog recertification, 2026-07-05:

- Active Requests contains no `window.prompt`, `window.confirm`, `prompt(`, or `confirm(` calls in the route, mobile, legacy density, request modal, details modal, emergency service, response service, or action utility slice.
- Payment retry saved-method selection uses shared `ModalShell` with route-owned radio choices and `retryPaymentWithDifferentMethod` as the receiver-backed submit.
- Single cancel and mark-complete use `ConfirmationModal`; bulk cancel/select remains excluded until destructive receiver authority, role scope, confirmation design, and app consequence are proved.
- Native browser dialogs are not reusable modal or confirmation proof for any future page. A future page must use `ModalShell`, `ConfirmationModal`, or an explicitly approved shared surface, or keep the action unavailable.

Requests visible-action recertification, 2026-07-05:

- Active Requests contains no `opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100`, `group-hover:opacity-100`, or `hover:opacity` action reveal pattern in the route or mobile surface.
- Desktop actions are visible in the focused detail rail: one full-width primary action from `getPrimaryRailAction()` plus visible secondary rail buttons for Dispatch, Complete, Retry, and Details when those receivers are allowed.
- Mobile actions appear after row reveal as visible `Details` plus the role-gated review action; there is no mobile destructive cancel shortcut.
- The old inactive list view still contains hover-reveal action chrome and cannot be reintroduced as active UI until it is converted to visible pointer, keyboard, and touch-safe actions.

### Requests Filter And State Canon - 2026-07-03

Requests filter/state behavior is now part of the reusable multi-data canon, with source and rendered proof.

- `FilterSheet` remains the shared filter shell. It owns ARIA dialog semantics, `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby`, Escape close, close button copy, route-specific `Clear`, and mobile bottom-island suppression.
- Desktop filters use a two-column adaptive sheet: search spans the sheet, status and date live side by side, and the footer actions stay visible without crowding the date controls.
- Mobile filters stay as a bottom sheet: status first, date range below, compact vertical scroll, bottom island hidden while open, and bottom island restored when the sheet closes.
- Filter action color is neutral for ordinary filtering. Red/brand-danger styling is not used for `Apply`.
- Date filters are real request filters now. `created_at.start` and `created_at.end` map to `date_from` and `date_to` before `getEmergencyRequestsPage()` runs.
- Empty and error states are separated. A failed load shows `Requests did not load` with `Retry`; an empty successful result shows `No matching requests` and points back to filters/search.
- Mobile receives the same load-error contract through `MobileEmergency`, using the shared mobile empty/recover pattern instead of a blank or stale render.
- The PWA install prompt is shell-owned and must yield to active work surfaces. It hides while any modal/dialog/filter sheet is open and rests above the mobile bottom island when visible.

Rendered proof completed on the reused local `3000` server:

- Desktop `/emergencies?proof=requests-filter-canon`: opened filter sheet, confirmed labeled dialog, typed search, used `Clear`, applied filters, verified the dialog closed, no horizontal overflow, and no warning/error console output.
- Mobile `/emergencies?proof=requests-filter-canon-mobile`: confirmed avatar plus bottom-island navigation, opened bottom-sheet filters, confirmed bottom island hidden while open, selected `Needs attention`, applied successfully after the install-prompt conflict fix, and verified bottom island restored.
- Browser screenshots were inspected during proof; temporary screenshot artifacts were removed after review so the repo does not collect local proof clutter.

Reuse decision: later pages may reuse this filter/state canon only after their own old-behavior ledger is complete. A page may not introduce private filter chrome, private empty/error copy, private modal stacking, or red ordinary filter actions.

Shared FilterSheet close repair, 2026-07-05:

- Rendered Requests proof found a closed `FilterSheet` could leave an invisible `role="dialog"` / `aria-modal="true"` surface with focus still on `Close filters`, because the exit spring did not complete.
- `FilterSheet.jsx` now returns `null` when closed and uses keyed direct `filter-sheet-backdrop` and `filter-sheet-shell` motion children plus bounded `filterBackdropTransition` and `filterSheetTransition` timings for the open path. Closed filter sheets must unmount instead of becoming invisible modal chrome.
- Reuse rule: any shared drawer, sheet, or modal must prove close/dismiss leaves no active dialog, no focus trap, no pointer-catching invisible surface, and no bottom-island suppression before the surface can be reused globally.

Shared ModalShell close repair, 2026-07-05:

- Fresh authenticated rendered proof was blocked by local sign-in rejecting the stored admin password, but the source audit found the same class of risk: `ModalShell.jsx` still used an `AnimatePresence` exit path that could leave shared dialog chrome alive while visually gone.
- `ModalShell.jsx` now returns `null` when closed and uses keyed direct `modal-shell-backdrop` and `modal-shell-surface` motion children plus bounded `modalBackdropTransition` and `modalShellTransition` timings for the open path. Closed modals must unmount instead of relying on exit animation cleanup.
- Reuse rule: canonical modals must share `ModalShell`, open with immediate visible feedback, and prove close/dismiss leaves no active `role="dialog"`, no hidden backdrop, no focus trap, and no lingering app-chrome suppression before a page can reuse the modal pattern.

### Requests State And Row Feedback Recertification - 2026-07-05

Requests remains the canonical multi-data pattern, so its first-glance state choices and scanning rows must expose explicit feedback state across desktop and mobile.

- The page now starts from a smart selected state. It first checks `pending` because `Needs attention` / `to review` means `pending_approval`; if pending is clear and active requests exist, it selects `active`; if no active work exists but historical service slices exist, it selects the largest useful service slice before falling back to pending.
- Pending and active are separate first-glance choices again, but with strict language: `pending` is the `Needs attention` decision point; `active` is non-terminal care activity and must not be described as review-needed work.
- Desktop state choices expose `data-request-kpi`, `data-state="selected|idle"`, `aria-pressed`, and a count-bearing label.
- Desktop request rows expose `data-request-row`, `data-state="selected|idle"`, and `aria-pressed` so the focused detail rail has a testable selected source.
- Mobile state choices use the same `data-request-kpi` and `data-state="selected|idle"` vocabulary. Mobile rows expose `data-mobile-request-row` and `data-state="expanded|idle"` for the row reveal pattern.

Reuse decision: later multi-data pages may reuse `selected`, `idle`, and `expanded` only after proving which controls filter the canonical query, which row owns the focused detail source, and which mobile row reveal is contextual rather than a separate modal.

Rendered proof, 2026-07-07:

- Initial desktop state now may start on `pending`, `active`, `bed`, or `ambulance` depending on route-owned stats. A dataset with `pending = 0` and `active > 0` must render the active request rows instead of an empty pending slice.
- Read-only Supabase proof with the local admin proof credentials reported `total = 168`, `pending = 0`, `active = 1`, `bed = 23`, and `ambulance = 145`. The active row was `REQ-6F7A0D`, status `accepted`, service `ambulance`.
- Mobile `/emergencies` rendered `Requests | iVisit Console`, `1 active request`, `Active` selected, `Needs attention: 0`, `Beds: 23`, `Ambulance: 145`, one active row, no framework overlay, and no warning/error logs after route navigation. Expanding the row exposed facility, location, service, and Details without mutating data.
- Desktop `1440x900` `/emergencies` rendered the same active state with one selected `data-request-row`, the right detail rail, no empty-state copy, and no framework overlay.
- Today `/` rendered the same semantic priority: pending approvals can own the page headline, but Requests is shown as `1 active`, not `1 request to review`, when `pending = 0` and `active > 0`.

### Requests Trigger Feedback Recertification - 2026-07-05

Requests trigger surfaces now share the same feedback vocabulary as its rows and state choices.

- Desktop header filter, desktop sheet filter, and empty-state `Change filters` triggers expose `data-state="idle|filtered|open"`, `aria-haspopup="dialog"`, and `aria-expanded` tied to the shared `FilterSheet`.
- Desktop `New request` exposes `data-state="idle|open"`, `aria-haspopup="dialog"`, and `aria-expanded` tied to the route-owned `EmergencyRequestModal`.
- Mobile filter uses the same `idle|filtered|open` filter state. Mobile request statistics exposes `idle|open` and dialog semantics tied to the route analytics modal.
- This is trigger feedback only. It does not change the Requests service owner, RBAC, create payload, analytics data, filter schema, pagination, realtime channel, or modal receiver.

Reuse decision: later pages may not copy Requests visual trigger placement without this state contract. Modal and filter triggers must name their receiver through ARIA, expose visible press/open state, and keep the page as the owner of the sheet/modal event.

Rendered proof, 2026-07-05:

- Desktop `/emergencies?proof=requests-trigger-feedback-2026-07-05` rendered `Requests | iVisit Console` with no framework overlay and no warning/error logs. Initial filter, `New request`, and sheet `Filters` triggers reported `data-state="idle"` with dialog semantics.
- Opening the desktop filter changed the header filter trigger to `data-state="open"` / `aria-expanded="true"` and rendered one shared `data-filter-sheet-shell="true"` dialog. Opening `New request` changed the trigger to `open` and rendered the route-owned shared modal without submitting data.
- Mobile `390x844` `/emergencies?proof=requests-trigger-feedback-mobile-2026-07-05` rendered filter and statistics triggers as `idle` with dialog semantics. Opening filter changed the trigger to `open`, rendered the shared filter dialog, and made the bottom island non-interactive while the sheet was open.
- Opening request statistics changed the mobile statistics trigger to `open` and rendered the shared analytics dialog with no warning/error logs. The follow-up modal recertification below cleans its copy, ordinary action color, and false fallback metrics before treating the reveal as part of the Requests canon.

### Shared Analytics Modal Recertification - 2026-07-05

Requests statistics opens the shared `AnalyticsModal`, so that modal now has to meet the Today/Requests language, color, and data-truth rules before later pages reuse it as a secondary reveal.

- `AnalyticsModal.jsx` now uses plain labels such as `Statistics`, `Summary`, `Priority`, `Status`, `Avg response`, `Needs review`, `Close`, and `Next`. It removes old command-room copy such as `Analytic Engine`, `Response Pulse`, `Priority Heat`, `Status Flow`, `Health Index`, `In-Flow`, `Signal processing`, and `No segments detected`.
- `AnalyticsModal.jsx` now uses shared `ModalShell` chrome for backdrop, ARIA dialog semantics, scroll containment, sticky footer framing, and mobile shell suppression instead of private fixed overlay/dialog markup.
- Ordinary modal chrome moved off the global primary/red treatment. Progress, phase labels, and chart bars use local sky information color; the main next action uses neutral foreground. Red remains available only for real danger values such as nonzero needs-review, rejected, expired, or emergency counts.
- The modal no longer invents `12.0m` response time from `avgResponseTime || 12`, and it no longer produces percentages from a denominator fallback of `1`. Missing response time or missing denominator renders `No data`.
- This is a shared reveal-surface cleanup only. `AnalyticsModal` remains a renderer for route-provided stats and does not become a data owner, report authority, export authority, or proof that an unadmitted page may reuse analytics.

Reuse decision: later pages may reuse `AnalyticsModal` only as a secondary reveal after their ledger proves route-owned stats, failed-read behavior, source/completeness labels, and role scope. The modal's clean chrome is reusable; its data is never authority by itself.

### Requests RBAC Resource Recertification - 2026-07-05

Requests route compatibility stays `/emergencies`, but the permission and backend resource name is now `emergency_requests`.

- `navigation.js` and `routes.jsx` use `resource: 'emergency_requests'` for Requests, matching the Supabase table and `emergencyService` owner.
- `AuthContext.can()` normalizes the old `emergencies` resource name to `emergency_requests` as a compatibility alias only. New page ledgers should name the backend resource, not the path id.
- Focused proof passed with `EmergencyRequestsPage.contract.test.js`, `AuthContext.contract.test.js`, and `PageRevampGate.contract.test.js`; the default UI hardgate and production build also passed after the source change.
- Rendered proof, 2026-07-05 recertification: the in-app Browser loaded `/emergencies?proof=requests-rbac-render-2026-07-05` in a fresh tab, settled to `Requests | iVisit Console`, rendered the shared shell plus Requests stage/rail empty state, showed no `Unexpected token '<'`, no `ChunkLoadError`, no framework overlay, and no warning/error logs. Opening the desktop `Filters` trigger resolved one control and displayed the shared FilterSheet with Search requests, Status, Date range, Clear, and Apply without runtime errors.

### Today/Requests Color And Feedback Recertification - 2026-07-03

Current source recertification tightened the color rule in the canonical pair:

- Red/brand-danger stays reserved for real request attention, destructive actions, emergency states, and failed loads.
- Today route actions use neutral foreground buttons, not brand-danger red. The role signal may show danger only when the role has real work needing attention.
- Today non-danger `primary` tone now uses the local sky semantic treatment instead of the global primary/red token.
- Requests filter triggers, active-filter indicators, empty-state filter recovery, mobile filter taps, and mobile statistics taps use neutral or sky feedback rather than red.
- Filter date presets and `Apply` stay ordinary actions. They do not use red unless a future page proves an emergency/destructive meaning.

Reuse decision: later pages may use neutral foreground for ordinary primary movement, local sky/cyan for information or selection, amber for waiting, emerald for clear/success, and red only for danger/error/emergency/destructive meaning.

### Today Sheet Interaction Recertification - 2026-07-05

Today remains the role-first first-glance page, but its controls now share the same explicit feedback vocabulary required by Requests and later admitted pages.

- Glance cards stay clickable route buttons with `data-state="idle|opening"` and a spinner while the selected route is opening.
- Each Today glance card renders a route CTA icon (`ArrowRight`) and mutates that icon to `Loader2` while opening, so Requests, Approvals, Staff, Visits, Impact, and setup cards never feel stale after tap.
- The primary sheet action now exposes `data-state="idle|opening"` and an opening label before navigation completes.
- Detail rows expose `data-state="idle|expanded|opening|unavailable"`, `aria-expanded`, and route-action opening feedback. Expanding a row is the progressive reveal step; executing a row action is a separate route-owned action.
- The viewer access row stays unavailable and names the reason: an admin must assign the role before the action is available.

Reuse decision: later pages may reuse this feedback vocabulary for cards, rows, and route actions only after their own old-behavior ledger proves which controls are real actions, which are progressive reveal, and which are unavailable.

Rendered proof, 2026-07-05:

- Today desktop at `/?proof=today-feedback-canon-2026-07-05` rendered `Today | iVisit Console` with no framework overlay, no horizontal overflow, and no warning/error logs.
- The primary `Review` action resolved as one control. Clicking it moved the primary action, Requests glance card, and Requests detail row to `data-state="opening"` with `Opening...` feedback before the route settled at `/emergencies`.
- The settled Requests route rendered `Requests | iVisit Console` with no framework overlay, no horizontal overflow, and no warning/error logs.

Rendered proof, 2026-07-04:

- Today desktop at `/?proof=today-color-canon` rendered `Today | iVisit Console` with no horizontal overflow. The route action used neutral foreground styling, glance cards had neutral focus feedback, and ordinary route buttons did not carry `bg-primary`.
- Requests desktop at `/emergencies?proof=requests-color-canon` rendered `Requests | iVisit Console` with neutral filter triggers, neutral `Change filters`, neutral `Apply`, neutral date presets, and no horizontal overflow.
- Requests mobile at `/emergencies?proof=requests-color-canon-mobile` rendered the avatar/bottom-island model, kept filter/stat controls neutral, hid the bottom island while the filter sheet was open, restored it after `Apply`, and had no horizontal overflow.
- The in-app browser automation API reset twice during local content polling, so rendered proof continued through the available Playwright browser control. Fresh proof console output had no warnings or errors; temporary screenshots were inspected and then removed.

### Runtime Proof Stability Recertification - 2026-07-05

Stale CRA chunk failures are proof stability failures, not page-domain truth. A red runtime overlay from `Unexpected token '<'`, `ChunkLoadError`, or `Loading chunk ... failed` does not authorize a page redesign or data-path rewrite until the stale asset path is ruled out.

- `public/index.html` installs an early capture-phase stale-asset guard before the framework overlay can render. It catches stale script errors, same-origin root `*.hot-update.js`, `*.hot-update.json`, `*.chunk.js`, and module-style `*.mjs`/`*.jsx` failures, dynamic import failures, `Unexpected token '<'`, and `expected expression, got '<'`, marks stale runtime errors for the Webpack overlay filter, exposes the same recovery hook to the dev-server overlay filter, then reloads the current route once through `ivisit-console:stale-asset-reload` with a cache-busting `__asset_refresh` query.
- `src/index.js` mirrors the guard after React boot. It suppresses only known Supabase auth refresh noise and stale asset failures, stops duplicate overlay propagation for stale chunks and hot updates, clears console-owned CacheStorage entries before the recovery refresh, reloads once per route, and shows a single `Refresh needed` fallback instead of letting the framework overlay render if the same stale asset failure repeats during cooldown.
- Local development unregisters service workers through `src/index.js`, and the HTML shell clears localhost registrations and console-owned CacheStorage entries before app boot. This keeps old precache entries from making the console look broken during long revamp runs.
- `ErrorBoundary.jsx` remains the human-facing fallback when recovery is exhausted. It labels stale chunk errors as `Refresh Needed` instead of a product failure and clears console-owned CacheStorage entries before refresh.
- `App.js` now uses a shared `RouteLoadingState` Suspense fallback instead of a blank shell. Lazy route transitions show `Loading page`, compact skeleton blocks, and stable sheet-like structure while route chunks resolve, so a slow or recovering page cannot look like an empty console. The fallback now uses canonical `rounded-card`, `rounded-inner`, and `rounded-icon` geometry, and the PWA debug chip uses `rounded-pill`, so shared route-loading chrome cannot drift away from the squircle system.
- Direct server proof on the reused `3000` dev server confirmed browser-like deep links return the app shell with the early guard installed.
- `craco.config.js` now restricts the dev-server history fallback to real HTML navigations. Missing JavaScript chunks, root hot updates, source maps, CSS, JSON, and other asset requests must return asset failures instead of `index.html`, preventing the browser from executing `<!doctype html>` as JavaScript. The dev server also inserts `ivisit-missing-runtime-asset-404` before `connect-history-api-fallback`, so stale same-origin JavaScript URLs, including missing `/static/js/*.mjs` and `/static/js/*.jsx`, receive a `404` with `application/javascript` and hot-update manifests receive `application/json` instead of an HTML error page. The local dev server also sends `Cache-Control: no-store, no-cache, must-revalidate, proxy-revalidate`, `Pragma: no-cache`, and `Expires: 0` on every response so rendered proof does not reuse an old app shell while hot updates are moving. The dev-server runtime overlay also delegates to the early HTML guard, so stale asset parse/load failures are filtered from the red overlay while ordinary runtime errors still render. The dev-server runtime overlay calls the same stale-asset recovery hook before filtering stale runtime overlays. The stale-asset guard now cache-busts with `__asset_refresh` and shows a single `Refresh needed` fallback instead of letting the framework overlay render when recovery is exhausted. The overlay filter also trusts the bare stale-asset message, so a `SyntaxError: Unexpected token '<'` reported without a chunk stack is treated as stale runtime recovery instead of a page-domain failure.
- Browser proof on `/visits` also exposed Supabase GoTrue navigator-lock `AbortError: signal is aborted without reason` noise on auth redirects. `public/index.html` and `src/index.js` now suppress only that exact auth-lock abort before the framework overlay can paint; this does not classify arbitrary aborts or page data failures as healthy.
- Restarted-server Browser proof on `/emergencies?proof=requests-after-restart-2026-07-05` confirmed the cleaned single dev server settled to `Requests | iVisit Console`, rendered the Requests main content, and showed no `Unexpected token '<'`, no `ChunkLoadError`, and no warning/error logs.
- Follow-up Browser proof on `/visits?proof=runtime-token-debug-2026-07-05` confirmed the reused single dev server rendered `Visits | iVisit Console` with no warning/error logs. Direct HTTP proof against the user's earlier Radix chunk URL returned `200 application/javascript`; missing chunk and stale hot-update URLs returned `404 application/javascript` with no-store headers, not HTML.
- Overlay follow-up on 2026-07-06 keeps the dev-server overlay self-contained: `ivisitConsoleRuntimeErrorOverlayFilter` now carries its own stale-asset detector and reload fallback, so a bare `SyntaxError: Unexpected token '<'` still cache-busts through `__asset_refresh` even if the early HTML hook is isolated, delayed, or unavailable in the overlay runtime.
- Browser error follow-up on 2026-07-06 chains `window.onerror` in both `public/index.html` and `src/index.js`. Raw same-origin script parse failures, including `SyntaxError: Unexpected token '<'`, now call the same stale-asset recovery path and return `true` to suppress browser/framework default handling, while non-stale errors keep flowing to the previous handler and the app error boundary.
- Stale HTML parse follow-up on 2026-07-06 promotes this into an explicit HTML-as-JS detector across `public/index.html`, `src/index.js`, `ErrorBoundary.jsx`, and `craco.config.js`. It matches the bare `Unexpected token '<'` form, longer token messages that include `<`, and `<!doctype html>` bodies, then routes them through the same single-reload/cache-bust path instead of leaving the Webpack runtime overlay stale.
- Localhost analytics follow-up on 2026-07-06 keeps PostHog out of local proof runs. `public/index.html` now returns before `posthog.init(...)` on `localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, and `::1`, so third-party analytics scripts cannot show up as misleading `Unexpected token '<'` overlays while Console pages are being verified locally. Production still initializes PostHog.
- Localhost payment-script follow-up on 2026-07-06 moved Stripe.js to the pure loader and makes `GlobalFinancialModals` call `loadStripe(...)` only after the Payment cards modal opens on an HTTPS origin. Stripe.js cannot load on unrelated local proof routes before the Payment cards modal opens, so payment-provider script failures cannot masquerade as page or stale-chunk failures during Requests/Visits/Analytics proof.

Reuse decision: rendered proof for future pages must reuse one local server when safe, check `netstat`, treat stale chunk overlays as runtime-proof issues first, and only then audit page source, design, or backend ownership.

### Reuse Rule

Do not copy the Requests markup into the rest of the app. Reuse the canon:

- Page signal model.
- KPI/state choice model.
- Handled sheet model.
- Focused detail rail model.
- Route-owned action model.
- Mobile avatar plus bottom-island model.
- ModalShell model.
- Quiet route-owned data model.
- Local semantic colors for non-danger states when global tokens are brand-red.

Each later page must still audit its own old behavior first. Reuse is allowed only after the page proves no working behavior, data path, workflow, or responsive state was lost.

Active Requests handled-sheet lock, 2026-07-05:

- The old Git-backed Requests page remains the preservation anchor for `useViewMode`, `ViewToggle`, `EmergencyRequestListView`, and `EmergencyRequestTableView`.
- The active Requests route does not import those legacy density surfaces. It renders one route-owned handled sheet through `RequestsDesktopWorkspace`, `RequestSignalPanel`, `RequestKpiStrip`, `RequestRow`, `RequestDetailRail`, and the mobile handled sheet in `MobileEmergency`.
- Legacy list/table/card density may be reintroduced only as variants of the same `getEmergencyRequestsPage()` projection after the page ledger records the behavior being preserved, the shared shell relationship, and the interaction/RBAC proof. It cannot return as a second request source, private view-mode store, or private page chrome.

Today/Requests data-ownership lock, 2026-07-05:

- Today at `/` is not a route-owned startup override. It intentionally consumes `PageDataContext` role-scoped summary data for first-glance routing, then sends users to the page that owns each command.
- Today cannot present PageData startup failure, mock fallback, or role-critical loading as complete truth. `TodayHome.jsx` keeps that state in `Live details are not ready` and route-action guidance.
- Requests at `/emergencies` is route-owned with a zero-domain startup override. `PageDataContext` must not load the `emergency` startup domain while the Requests route is open.
- Active Requests cannot consume `PageDataContext` emergency stats for KPI, signal, mobile, analytics, or handled-sheet rendering. It uses `getEmergencyRequestsPage()` and `getEmergencyRequestsPageStats()` as the route read/stat owners.

Today right-panel route-context lock, 2026-07-06:

- `TodayHome.jsx` publishes `todayPanelContext` with role scope, live/loading/error truth, glance cards, rows, counts, route feedback state, and the existing navigation handler.
- `ContextPanel.jsx` requests that context with `requestTodayRouteContext` and passes `<DashboardPanel todayContext={todayRouteContext} />`.
- `DashboardPanel.jsx` consumes `todayContext`, not `usePageData`; uses simple labels `Today overview`, `Current route scope`, `Panel actions`, and `Current list`; exposes local `role="status"` / `aria-live="polite"` feedback; removes stale `Quick Actions`, refresh/export/backup controls, local alert thresholds, and old global dashboard data props; and is now in the default UI hardgate.
- Active Today no longer lets the shared right panel duplicate PageData summary truth or own command-like dashboard controls. The panel can only ask Today to navigate to the route that already owns the work.

Requests right-panel route-context lock, 2026-07-06:

- The shared desktop right panel now follows the same page-to-layout rule as the handled sheet. `EmergencyRequestsPage.jsx` publishes `requestPanelContext` from the active route rows, stats, focused request, loading/error state, current state choice, filter state, and create-role flag.
- `ContextPanel.jsx` requests that context with `requestEmergencyRouteContext` and passes `<EmergencyPanel requestContext={emergencyRouteContext} />`; it no longer passes `PageDataContext` emergency rows into the Requests panel.
- `EmergencyPanel.jsx` consumes `requestContext`, not `usePageData`; uses simple labels `Requests overview`, `Current route scope`, `Panel actions`, and `Current list`; keeps New/Filter/Stats as route-owned shell bridges; exposes local `role="status"` / `aria-live="polite"` feedback; and is now in the default UI hardgate.
- This preserves the old right-panel action inventory while closing the duplicate data-owner path. It does not make the event bridge reusable globally; the future page-actions context should replace those window events when that pass starts.

Today/Requests squircle admission lock, 2026-07-06:

- Global CSS remains the radius owner: `src/index.css` defines the iVisit squircle tokens and semantic aliases, and `tailwind.config.js` exposes `rounded-sheet`, `rounded-card`, `rounded-inner`, `rounded-icon`, `rounded-button`, `rounded-pill`, `rounded-modal`, and `rounded-squircle`.
- Legacy size aliases such as `squircle-xl` and `squircle-sm` remain only as migration shims in global CSS. Candidate revamp files must use role-based tokens like `rounded-card`, `rounded-inner`, `rounded-icon`, `rounded-button`, `rounded-pill`, `rounded-modal`, and `rounded-sheet`.
- The active Today pair (`TodayHome.jsx`, `DashboardPanel.jsx`) and active Requests slice (`EmergencyRequestsPage.jsx`, `MobileEmergency.jsx`, `EmergencyPanel.jsx`, `EmergencyDetailsModal.jsx`, `EmergencyRequestModal.jsx`) now pass `node scripts/check-ui-surface-hardgate.js --strict-radius ...`.
- `App.js` route loading fallback uses `rounded-card`, `rounded-inner`, `rounded-icon`, and `rounded-pill` and is part of default hardgate coverage.
- The shell FAB and mobile island drift found on 2026-07-06 was corrected: `ContextAwareFAB.jsx` uses `rounded-button` for the icon surface and `rounded-pill` for its tooltip; `DynamicBottomBar.jsx` uses `rounded-pill` for the island and action FAB, and `rounded-button` for nav item controls. Both shared shell components now pass the same strict radius gate.
- These files may use only role-based radius utilities. Full sheets use `rounded-sheet`, entity cards use `rounded-card`, nested surfaces use `rounded-inner`, icon/avatar wells use `rounded-icon`, controls use `rounded-button`, badges/handles use `rounded-pill`, and modal-like nested shells use `rounded-modal`.
- Candidate pages cannot be admitted into the revamp canon while using generic or arbitrary radius utilities such as `rounded-full`, `rounded-2xl`, `rounded-3xl`, or `rounded-[...]`. Convert the page to the semantic tokens before the visual review can pass.
- Global voice lock: `.bg-orb` is retired. Auth and app pages should use calm system backgrounds, surface depth, and route-owned media only; the UI hardgate rejects `bg-orb` in future revamp candidates.
- Global typography lock: `CONSOLE_DESIGN_SYSTEM_FROM_APP.md` examples now use plain readable labels, normal letter spacing, and role-based squircle tokens. Old all-caps/tracking chrome is not a pattern source for new page revamps.
- Global chrome lock: active design-system examples avoid decorative Tailwind border, ring, outline, divider, and hairline utilities. Surface separation should come from spacing, tone, depth, and nested squircle surfaces.

Today/Requests private shell chrome lock, 2026-07-05:

- Today and Requests consume shell state through `usePageHeader`, `usePageFooter`, and `usePageShell`. They do not import shared shell owners such as `SmartHeader`, `ResponsiveSidebar`, `IslandNavigation`, `DynamicBottomBar`, `ContextAwareFAB`, `NotificationCenter`, `MobileNavMenu`, `ContextPanelShell`, or `SmartFooter`.
- Page-local surfaces are allowed only when mapped in the ledger and backed by the shared component system: `ConsoleModuleRail`, `FilterSheet`, `ModalShell`, `RequestDetailRail`, and the mobile handled sheet in `MobileEmergency`.
- Future pages may reuse this shell relationship only through shared shell APIs or a ledger-approved exception. A private header, sidebar, notification dropdown, mobile menu, footer, FAB, or right-panel shell fails the page-to-layout gate before visual review.

Today/Requests route-owned action duplication lock, 2026-07-05:

- `ContextAwareFAB.jsx` and `DynamicBottomBar.jsx` both treat `/` and `/emergencies` as route-owned action surfaces, so the generic global FAB cannot duplicate Today cards or Requests actions.
- Requests mobile may show one route-owned `New request` action through `getRouteOwnedMobileAction()` and `openEmergencyModal`. Today has no shell-level mobile FAB because the first-glance cards and primary CTA own the next action.
- Future pages that use the Today/Requests canon must choose: either route-owned page action, route-owned mobile action, or generic context action. They cannot show two competing action models for the same route.

Today/Requests state-pattern lock, 2026-07-05:

- Today treats role-critical loading, PageData domain failure, or mock fallback as not-ready truth: `Live details are not ready`, `Retry needed`, and row-level `unavailable`, `opening`, or `expanded` states. It must not show clear/all-clear copy while the role-critical summary is incomplete.
- Requests separates structural loading, full failed load, stale refresh failure with existing rows, true empty filters, selected rows, opened filters, and modal-open feedback. `RequestSkeletonRows`, `RequestLoadErrorState`, `RequestLoadNotice`, `No matching requests`, `Change filters`, `data-state`, `aria-pressed`, `aria-expanded`, and retry controls are the desktop reference.
- Requests mobile mirrors the same state contract with `MobileListSkeletonRows`, `MobileListEmpty`, `MobileListLoadMore`, pull-to-refresh, filter/statistics open state, KPI selected state, and row `expanded`/`idle` state.
- Future pages must map loading, empty, error, unavailable, selected, opening, pending, and retry states before visual implementation. A page cannot turn failed, capped, loading, or filtered data into complete truth.

Today/Requests action-authority lock, 2026-07-05:

- Today is navigation-only. `TodayHome.jsx` can set route-transition feedback and call `navigate(path)`, but it must not import Supabase, Requests service commands, dispatch/complete/cancel/payment receivers, or toast success copy. Today sends users to the page that owns each command.
- Requests is receiver-backed. Create opens the route-owned `EmergencyRequestModal` for admin/org-admin users; dispatch uses `dispatchEmergency`; completion uses `completeEmergency`; payment retry uses `retryPaymentWithDifferentMethod`; cancellation uses `cancelEmergencyRequest`; all stay behind `getEmergencyActionState()` and role checks.
- Requests must show immediate action feedback through `data-state`, modal open state, confirmation modal, or toast loading/success/error. A disabled or unavailable action must refresh or explain why it is not ready.
- Cash settlement remains unavailable in Requests until the finance receiver pass proves authority. Future pages must keep receiver, role, payload, pending feedback, success/failure feedback, and app consequence named before any action remains enabled.

Today/Requests realtime and side-effect lock, 2026-07-05:

- Today has no page-owned realtime, direct Supabase reads/writes, or domain side-effect owner. Its only window event listener is the route-context publisher that feeds the shared right panel and cleans itself up with `removeEventListener`. It may use the short route-transition timer in `handleAction()` only to acknowledge navigation before the destination route takes over.
- Requests preserves the old Git-backed realtime behavior as a route-owned refresh path only: one `supabase.channel('emergency_changes')` in `EmergencyRequestsPage.jsx`, scoped to `emergency_requests` and `payments`, both calling `fetchRequests`, with `return () => supabase.removeChannel(channel);` cleanup on unmount.
- Requests guards concurrent route refreshes with `requestSeqRef`. Stale responses must return before mutating rows, stats, loading, or error state; `isTransientRequestRefreshError()` may suppress only transient stale/abort refresh noise after the sequence check, not real page failure.
- Requests still has a legacy shell action bridge for route-owned openings: `openEmergencyModal`, `openFilters`, and `openAnalyticsModal`. Each listener must have a matching `removeEventListener` cleanup until the future page-action context replaces the bridge.
- Future pages must name startup domains, direct Supabase/Auth/Edge/Storage call sites, realtime channel owner, event-bus/timer owner, cleanup behavior, stale-response behavior, and denied/failed feedback before any side effect remains active.

Today/Requests query-scope lock, 2026-07-05:

- Today may display role-scoped summary counts from `PageDataContext` only as first-glance routing guidance. It must not turn those summaries into a local list, exact count, pagination, export, or workflow command owner.
- Cross-page request language is locked: `Needs attention`, `to review`, and `Review requests` mean `pending_approval`; `active` means non-terminal care activity and must not be described as review-needed work.
- Requests owns multi-data query scope through `getEmergencyRequestsPage()`: `buildRequestsServiceFilter()` maps UI `status`, `search`, `date_from`, and `date_to`; the route passes `kpiFilter`, `limit`, `offset`, `sortKey`, and `sortDirection`; the page writes `pagination.setTotalCount(count || 0)` from the service result.
- `emergencyService.js` owns exact counts through `getEmergencyPageExactCount()` with `count: 'exact'` and `head: true`; page rows are loaded with `.range(offset, offset + limit - 1)` and an allowlisted sort key. Stats reuse exact count queries instead of visible-row counts.
- The Requests sheet renders `PaginationControls` from the service-backed `totalCount` and `itemsPerPage`. Empty filter state is based on `Number(pagination.totalCount) === 0`, not a failed read, capped page, or current visible row length.
- Regression guard from 2026-07-07: an empty UI status array (`status: []`) once reached Supabase as an equality filter and made Requests render zero rows while the backend still had data. Every route data owner must prove cleared filters do not become backend constraints, and rendered proof for multi-data pages must compare at least one known nonzero backend slice against the visible KPI/list state.
- Future pages must name exact count owner, filter normalization, date bounds, sort allowlist, pagination owner, export scope, and whether the visible rows are complete or merely the current page before a table, sheet, or analytics reveal can claim complete truth.

### Requests Pattern Extraction Contract

When a later page says it follows Requests, it must map its old behavior into these slots before UI work starts. The slot may be reused, adapted, excluded, or marked legacy inactive, but it cannot be skipped.

| Requests pattern slot | What may be reused | Required proof before reuse |
|---|---|---|
| Signal field | One first-glance sentence, one support line, one status chip, and local semantic color. | The page names the route data/stat owner, proves counts are exact or honestly limited, and shows failed/loading truth as not-ready instead of clear. |
| State choices | 2-4 clickable filters, KPI choices, or workflow states tied to the same projection. | Each choice maps to a service query/filter or a documented local projection of service-owned rows; selected/pressed state is visible. |
| Filter normalization | UI filter defaults, clear/reset state, date bounds, search terms, and status arrays normalized before the backend query. | Empty arrays, blank strings, empty date objects, and cleared defaults do not become backend constraints; focused tests or contracts cover the cleared-filter path and one known nonzero slice. |
| Handled sheet | The main scan surface for rows, cards, or table variants. | Old list/table/card behavior is classified as preserved, converted, excluded, or legacy inactive; no second source of truth remains. |
| Focused detail | A right rail, selected row reveal, or context panel with one primary next action. | The selected object is derived from the active projection; secondary actions stay hidden/unavailable until receiver authority and app consequence are proved. |
| Route-owned action | A single create/review/manage action in the header, page, or mobile FAB. | The role gate, receiver owner, submitted payload, pending feedback, success/failure feedback, and app consequence are named. |
| Shared modal/sheet | `ModalShell`, `FilterSheet`, `AnalyticsModal`, or a documented canonical exception. | The surface has ARIA dialog semantics where needed, suppresses conflicting shell chrome on mobile, avoids private page backdrop/filter/dropdown chrome, and proves close/dismiss unmounts shared chrome with no active dialog, hidden backdrop, focus trap, pointer-catching invisible surface, or lingering bottom-island suppression. |
| Mobile recomposition | Avatar/account sheet, bottom island, signal first, compact choices, handled bottom sheet, row reveal, and route-owned action when allowed. | Mobile removes desktop-only choice density, keeps the first useful action visible, and proves no hamburger/private menu or bottom-bar/modal overlap. |
| Data quieting | Route-owned reads with global providers asleep unless they own the route domain. | The page names direct Supabase/Auth/Edge/Storage call sites, startup domains, realtime owner, cleanup behavior, and failed/denied state. |
| Local semantic color | Emerald for clear/success, amber for waiting, sky/cyan for ordinary selection/information, red only for danger/error/emergency/destructive meaning. | A color audit proves ordinary filters, empty recovery, selected rows, and primary movement do not use red/brand-danger styling. |
| Interaction feedback | Pressed, selected, opening, pending, disabled, loading, toast, or route transition feedback. | Every clickable card, row, filter, modal trigger, submit, pagination action, and route action has immediate visible acknowledgement. |

Reusable pattern proof is complete only when the page ledger includes this slot map, source contracts cover the slot decisions, the active page files are in the UI hardgate when visual work starts, and desktop plus mobile rendered proof confirms the page behaves like the mapped contract.

### Canonical Page Ledger Template

Each page ledger must answer these items before design implementation starts:

| Gate item | Required evidence |
|---|---|
| Old behavior inventory | `git show HEAD:<page>` or named commit evidence for prior card/list/table views, modals, filters, side effects, subscriptions, pagination, responsive views, empty/loading/error states, and actions. |
| Current source owner | Current route, service, hook/context, shell config, mobile component, modal, table/list/card component, and action receiver paths. |
| Page-to-shell contract | Whether the page is normal content, full-bleed stage, canvas, table workspace, or modal-led; which shell regions are visible; which element owns scroll. |
| RBAC/action authority | Visible action by role, disabled/unavailable action by role, receiver owner, command type, payload shape, authorization expectation, and app consequence. |
| Data rendering contract | Source truth, exact vs capped count, filter/search/sort owner, pagination owner, realtime owner, degraded state, and whether local UI state can ever be mistaken for backend truth. |
| Responsive contract | Desktop, tablet, 390px mobile, and wide desktop layout behavior; mobile must recomposed into fewer choices rather than shrink desktop chrome. |
| Canonical component use | Header, sidebar/rail, bottom island, footer, context panel, FAB, modal, drawer, dropdown, filter, tab, card, table, empty/loading/error state, notification, and route transition pattern. |
| Preservation decision | For every old behavior: `preserved`, `converted`, `excluded until receiver proof`, or `legacy inactive`. No silent drops. |
| QA proof | Contract tests, UI hardgate, build, encoding check, desktop proof, mobile proof, interaction feedback proof, and local process cleanup. |

### Canonical Pair Audit Matrix - Today And Requests

This matrix is the reusable reference before any other page claims the new console pattern.

| Area | Today canonical | Requests canonical | Reuse rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| Product role | First-glance role home: one next action for the current role. | Multi-data work stage: one live operational list with focused detail. | Decide whether the page is a first-glance home or multi-data work stage before drawing UI. |
| Shell relation | `TodayHome` uses `usePageShell({ bleed: true, hideFab: true })`, `usePageHeader('Today')`, hidden footer, shared rail, mobile bottom island, and a route-owned context payload for `DashboardPanel`. | `EmergencyRequestsPage` uses the same bleed/hide-FAB shell, shared rail with `Requests` active, route header action, and route-owned detail rail. | Pages consume shell. They do not create private app chrome or duplicate right-panel data ownership. |
| Data owner | `PageDataContext` supplies role-scoped summary data; route actions only navigate to owned pages. | `getEmergencyRequestsPage()` owns reads, exact count, pagination, filters, payment enrichment, row normalization, and degraded states. | Page data must have one named owner; global providers stay quiet unless that route owns the startup domain. |
| Old Git behavior | Old `BentoHome` was an Overview/Dashboard bento surface with role widgets, patient mobile dashboard, footer metrics, and broad PageData consumption. | Old `EmergencyRequestsPage` had grid/list/table modes, view toggle, filters, analytics modal, bulk selection, cash modal, retry modal, details/create/confirmation modals, pagination, realtime, and mobile pull-to-refresh/infinite load. | The ledger must map each old behavior to kept, converted, excluded, or legacy inactive. |
| Current conversion | Dashboard widgets became a role signal, 2-3 glance actions, a handled sheet, and one route action. Patient product behavior remains separate. | View modes became a single stage/sheet/detail flow; filters, analytics, create/details/confirmation/retry modals, pagination, realtime, and mobile refresh/load remain active. | Convert visual complexity into progressive disclosure, not into missing function. |
| Exclusions | Today does not perform high-risk writes; it points users to the page that owns the command. | Bulk cancel/select and cash settlement stay out of the active stage until destructive/finance receiver proof and app consequence are complete. Legacy list/table files are inactive until re-audited. | Excluded work must be honest and named. Never leave a clickable no-op. |
| Visual system | Atlas signal field, soft depth, simple role copy, actionable cards, no decorative container. | Same atlas/signal/sheet language, local semantic colors for non-danger states, status-aware rows, one detail rail. | Reuse stage/sheet language and semantic color rules, not copied markup. |
| Mobile system | Avatar opens account/overflow, bottom island owns role-primary nav, no hamburger, one action path. | Same avatar/bottom island; signal first, 2x2 state choices, handled bottom sheet, row reveal, route action only when allowed. | Mobile is recomposed into the next useful action; desktop controls are not merely squeezed. |
| Feedback system | Glance cards are buttons with pressed/opening states and immediate route feedback. | KPI choices, rows, modal triggers, dispatch/complete/retry paths, pagination, and mobile reveal provide immediate state feedback. | Every tap, hover, submit, route, and reveal needs visible acknowledgement. |
| Proof status | Page 1 ledger remains the role/RBAC and first-glance reference. | Page 2 ledger remains the multi-data and Requests parity reference. | Before touching another page, re-open Today and Requests side by side and confirm the canonical pair still matches. |

### Today/Requests Current-Source Reaudit - 2026-07-06

This pass re-opened the current worktree after the July 6 runtime and right-panel cleanups. The audit does not replace the old Git-backed preservation anchor; it proves the active source still matches the canon before another page inherits it.

- Targeted recent-history check for Today, Requests, shell, route panel, mobile navigation, and gate files returned no newer committed changes since `2026-06-28`. The preservation baseline therefore remains committed `HEAD` at `f31f29f`, and the active canon is current uncommitted worktree source on top of that baseline.
- Active Today source remains the role-first page. `TodayHome.jsx` consumes `usePageData()` for role-scoped first-glance summary only, checks role-critical `loading`, `domainErrors`, and `useMockData`, renders not-ready copy instead of clear/all-clear truth, uses `usePageHeader('Today')`, `usePageFooter(null, 'status', false)`, and `usePageShell({ bleed: true, hideFab: true })`, and publishes `todayPanelContext` for the shared right panel.
- Active Today right-panel source stays route-context owned. `ContextPanel.jsx` requests `requestTodayRouteContext` and passes `<DashboardPanel todayContext={todayRouteContext} />`; `DashboardPanel.jsx` consumes `todayContext`, exposes `role="status"` / `aria-live="polite"` feedback, and asks Today to navigate instead of reading global dashboard data or owning backup/export/refresh-style commands.
- Active Requests source remains the multi-data reference. `EmergencyRequestsPage.jsx` calls `getEmergencyRequestsPage()`, builds service filters from route UI state, writes `pagination.setTotalCount(count || 0)` from the service result, renders `FilterSheet`, `ModalShell`, `AnalyticsModal`, `ConfirmationModal`, `MobileEmergency`, `RequestsDesktopWorkspace`, `RequestSignalPanel`, `RequestKpiStrip`, `RequestRow`, and `RequestDetailRail`, and keeps old list/table density surfaces out of the active renderer.
- Active Requests right-panel source stays route-context owned. `EmergencyRequestsPage.jsx` publishes `requestPanelContext`; `ContextPanel.jsx` requests `requestEmergencyRouteContext` and passes `<EmergencyPanel requestContext={emergencyRouteContext} />`; `EmergencyPanel.jsx` consumes route context, exposes local status feedback, and keeps New, Filter, and Stats as route-owned shell bridges until the future page-actions context replaces the event bridge.
- Active mobile/action source still matches the canon. `mobileNavigation.js` keeps `overflowOwner: 'avatar'`, `bottomMenuButton: false`, and fixed role slots. `DynamicBottomBar.jsx` uses those slots plus one route-owned mobile action where allowed, while `ContextAwareFAB.jsx` hides the generic FAB on Today, Requests, and other route-owned action pages.
- Runtime proof stability remains a separate gate. The July 6 Analytics slice-default repair proves `/analytics` can render missing subscriber/hospital/mobile slices without a `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'total')` overlay, but Analytics stays Page 13 intake-only. A runtime fix on an unadmitted page cannot promote that page or broaden the Requests canon.
- Continuation rule: the next page must cite both the current-source reaudit and the old `HEAD` behavior inventory. If source drift changes shell ownership, route context, mobile navigation, event cleanup, state feedback, or data ownership, pause expansion and update this canon before implementing another page.

### Next Page Admission Gate

Before any later page may inherit Requests patterns or enter visual implementation, complete this admission check:

1. Name the page, route, minimum role, visible roles, and blocked roles.
2. Read the old Git-backed page and list every prior view, modal, action, filter, subscription, side effect, pagination path, empty state, loading state, error state, and responsive state.
3. Read the current page, service, hook/context, mobile component, modal, list/table/card component, route config, navigation config, and tests.
4. Classify every old behavior as `preserved`, `converted`, `excluded until receiver proof`, or `legacy inactive`.
5. Declare the page-to-shell contract: normal content, full-bleed stage, canvas, table workspace, or modal-led workflow.
6. Declare the data owner and prevent unrelated global providers from loading protected route data.
7. Declare the one primary action for the first screen and the progressive reveal path for secondary actions.
8. Decide which Requests canon applies: signal field, KPI/state choices, handled sheet, focused detail rail, route-owned action, mobile avatar plus bottom island, shared modal/sheet with close-unmount proof, quiet route-owned data, or local semantic colors.
9. Add active page files to `scripts/check-ui-surface-hardgate.js` when visual implementation starts.
10. Run focused contract proof, UI hardgate, build, encoding checks, desktop proof, mobile proof, interaction feedback proof, and local process cleanup before the page can be considered complete.

If any item is missing, the page may stay in audit/planning but cannot be promoted as a revamped page.

Analytics intake decisions:

- Data ownership: not admitted. The active page no longer owns direct multi-table reads; `analyticsService.getAnalyticsIntakePage()` is the first intake read owner for the route. The page still computes several aggregates locally, and the service projection is a preserved intake wrapper rather than a final role-safe analytics projection owner. A future pass must define actor-specific provider, org-admin, sponsor, and admin analytics projections before using the data as complete operational truth.
- RBAC: not admitted. The route is provider-visible. The first cleanup now prevents non-admin subscriber analytics reads and org-admin finance reads, but finance summaries, user totals, sponsor/admin slices, denied states, and exact projection ownership still need explicit actor scope and receiver proof. Console must not expose admin-only subscriber or finance truth to provider/sponsor surfaces by visual implication.
- Export/report: blocked and fail-closed in active UI. Desktop export, mobile report generation, and `AnalyticsPanel` export events are preserved as old behavior inventory only. Active controls now provide unavailable feedback instead of a download or success state. A future action may remain enabled only after actor, dataset, time window, completeness, redaction, degraded state, file contents, and `source truth -> receiver -> app consequence` are proved.
- Predictive fallback and fixed copy: blocked as operational truth. Generated request volume, response, status, type, heatmap, `LIVE`, `READY`, estimated-baseline banners, fixed response deltas, and fixed finance progress may not be presented as measured analytics in the visual pass. The active cleanups removed the generated fallback path, relabelled unresolved desktop/mobile analytics as `Source pending`, and made finance/reporting progress measured-only; future work still must prove a role-safe analytics projection before visual admission.
- PageData/shell ownership: partially quieted, not admitted. `PageDataContext` still owns global summary analytics for eligible roles away from `/analytics`, while `/analytics` is now route-owned in `pageDataAccess.js` so global startup analytics does not duplicate the active route. The shell summary no longer carries the old `95` success-rate fallback or 30% on-route fleet estimate. `ContextPanel.jsx` no longer passes shell `analyticsData` into the `/analytics` right panel. This is a startup and shell-prop boundary cleanup only; it is not a canonical analytics source owner.
- Shared modal/panel: intake only. `AnalyticsModal` remains a render surface, not data authority. Current `AnalyticsPanel` is now a source-pending/unavailable surface only; old report/export events remain HEAD inventory and must not be reused until scope, receiver, and report authority are proved.
- Hardgate: intentionally absent. `Analytics.jsx`, `MobileAnalytics.jsx`, and `AnalyticsPanel.jsx` stay out of `scripts/check-ui-surface-hardgate.js` until the blocker map below is closed.

Required before Page 13 can move past intake:

1. Define one analytics projection owner per actor scope, including provider, org admin, sponsor, and admin. The first `getAnalyticsIntakePage()` cleanup centralizes active route acquisition only; it does not close final actor-scope projection ownership.
2. Remove or mark generated fallback analytics as unavailable/demo/source-pending before any rendered proof.
3. Keep desktop export, mobile report, and panel export unavailable until a scope-aware report/export projection is proved.
4. Prove subscription analytics and finance slices cannot leak beyond their role authority. The first cleanup now gates subscriber reads to admin and finance reads to admin/sponsor, but full sponsor/admin finance semantics, denied states, and projection ownership remain unproved.
5. Keep `/analytics` route-owned for global PageData startup quieting or replace it with a formal analytics projection owner; do not restore duplicate PageData summary acquisition on the active route.
6. Finish structural loading, failed, denied, partial, and projection-owner degraded states. The first cleanups now cover retryable first-load failures, source-slice partial/denied banners, and stale-refresh preservation on desktop and mobile, but final degraded semantics still need proof under the actor-specific projection owner before admission.
7. Add Page 13 hardgate coverage only when visual implementation starts.

Users intake decisions:

- Data ownership: not admitted. The route mixes profile service reads, Auth-enriched RPC reads, direct `profiles` count reads, local filtering/sorting/slicing, organization mapping, and route-published context summaries. PageData startup and shell recent-profile fetches are quieted on `/users`, but a future pass must name one scoped identity/user-registry projection owner before counts, search, filters, and mobile metrics are complete truth.
- RBAC and role authority: not admitted. The route is org-admin visible, but role mutation, provider type mutation, organization assignment, BVN verification, admin creation, and self/edit fields need receiver, RLS/RPC, audit, and app consequence proof.
- Invite and creation: blocked and fail-closed in active entry points. The dormant invite modal code invokes `invite-user` directly, has unproved deployed slug/email delivery semantics, and can package facility identity as organization scope. Manual create throws without an Auth identity id and is preserved as inventory only. Active header, URL, context-panel, and old global context action triggers now show unavailable feedback instead of opening invite/create modals.
- Destructive commands: blocked and fail-closed in active UI. Single and bulk delete previously called `delete_user_by_admin` directly from the page; active Users now converts those attempts to unavailable feedback. Delete may become enabled only behind a named identity/admin command owner with role scope, audit consequence, reflected read, and failure state.
- PageData and context panel: partially quieted, not admitted. `/users` is route-owned for startup data, `ContextPanel.jsx` reads the page-published Users route context, and `UsersPanel.jsx` does not fetch recent profiles when route context is supplied. The visible route still needs a single proved identity projection owner before admission.
- Shared modal/panel: intake only. `UserModal` and dormant `InviteUserModal` now use `ModalShell`, but invite still has direct receiver invocation and false-delivery risk. `UsersPanel` is a shell context surface, not user-registry authority.
- Mobile recomposition: not admitted. `MobileUsers` preserves pull-to-refresh, infinite load, KPI strip, featured metric, search/filter, analytics trigger, row reveal, details/edit, and selection inventory. Active mobile now removes generated chart/trend data, `LIVE` fallback, and the visible delete button, but it still needs a scoped identity projection before visible-row metrics can become complete truth.
- Hardgate: intentionally absent from the default set. `UsersPage.jsx`, `MobileUsers.jsx`, `UsersPanel.jsx`, `UserListView.jsx`, `UserTableView.jsx`, and `InviteUserModal.jsx` have focused strict-radius proof, but they stay out of `scripts/check-ui-surface-hardgate.js` until the blocker map below is closed or the route enters a guarded implementation pass with rendered proof and source/command ownership.

Required before Page 14 can move past intake:

1. Define a scoped identity/user-registry projection owner for list rows, exact count, role filters, search, sorting, pagination, recent activity, and aggregate stats.
2. Promote the route-owned Users context cleanup into a final identity projection only after read owner, exact count, pagination, stale/error states, and role scope are proved.
3. Prove invite receiver slug, authentication, submitted fields, email/link delivery truth, role grant semantics, organization/facility assignment, and failure states before any invite success copy remains enabled.
4. Prove role mutation, provider type mutation, organization assignment, BVN verification, and profile edit payload fields against the actual admin receiver/RLS/RPC and app consequence.
5. Move direct `delete_user_by_admin` single and bulk calls behind a named identity/admin command owner with audit/reflected-read proof, or keep delete unavailable.
6. Replace local `1000`-row slicing, local role counts, direct BVN count, and mobile visible-row fallback metrics with projection-backed complete/current-window labels.
7. Resolve the final action model: page header action, route-owned mobile action, context-panel action, or generic FAB. `/users` no longer opens competing `Add User` entry points, but the future visual pass still must choose one proved action model before any invite/create control is enabled.
8. Keep `InviteUserModal` dormant behind unavailable invite/create entry points until the receiver contract is truthful; shared `ModalShell` chrome does not imply command authority.
9. Keep the repaired mounted identity-row encoding in `UserListView` under contract before rendered desktop list proof closes.
10. Add rendered desktop, tablet, and mobile proof plus hardgate coverage only when visual implementation starts.

### Page 15 Intake Audit - Organizations

Audit status: started on 2026-07-05. Decision: Organizations at `/organizations` is intake only and is not admitted under the Today/Requests canon. No visual revamp, shared Requests pattern reuse, organization create/edit/delete enablement, wallet/billing metric promotion, route-owned action promotion, or hardgate promotion is authorized yet.

Why Organizations is Page 15:

- `/organizations` is an authenticated platform-admin route behind `ProtectedRoute minRole="admin"`.
- It is visible in desktop Admin navigation as `Organizations`; it has no role-primary mobile bottom-island entry.
- Pass 4 classifies organizations as part of the identity, verification, onboarding, auth, and settings authority chain.
- The route crosses multiple source owners: `organizations`, `organization_wallets`, `hospitals`, `profiles.organization_id`, Stripe/account fields, PageData shell summaries, right context panel actions, and finance/facility consequences owned by Pass 2 and Pass 3.

Pass authority evidence:

- `STAGE_5_FULL_SERVICE_COVERAGE_AUDIT_2026-05-24.md` says PageData startup and organization realtime call `getOrganizations()`, which loads all organizations and all organization wallets, then derives total/active/wallet values. That unbounded registry plus financial join cannot serve global shell totals.

That cannot prove organization identity or wallet initialization.

- Pass 4 also blocks `/organizations` desktop and mobile registry until organization rows, wallet preview, aggregate basis, CRUD readiness, and renderer command capability move to an organization projection. It specifically says direct create/update/delete and hard-coded or collection-derived health/finance claims must not remain enabled until receiver authority is proved.

- Data ownership: not admitted. - PageData and context panel: partially quieted, not admitted. `/organizations` is route-owned in `pageDataAccess.js`, so `PageDataContext` does not duplicate organization startup reads on the route. `ContextPanel.jsx` consumes the page-published Organizations route context instead of `PageDataContext` `organizationsData`. ### Page 16 Intake Audit - Settings

Audit status: started on 2026-07-06. Decision: Settings at `/settings` is intake only and is not admitted under the Today/Requests canon. No visual revamp, shared Requests pattern reuse, profile/security/support/billing enablement, provider self-service promotion, route-owned action promotion, or hardgate promotion is authorized yet.

Why Settings is Page 16:

- `/settings` is an authenticated own-user route behind `ProtectedRoute` with no route-level `minRole` prop in `App.js`, while dormant `routes.jsx` and account navigation classify it as `minRole: 'viewer'`.
- It is visible through desktop Account navigation and through viewer/sponsor mobile bottom navigation.
- The original route/nav/panel mismatch treated `/settings` as admin context even though the route and nav expose own-user settings to viewer-level actors. The first shell alignment cleanup now treats `/settings` as an own-user context panel, but final action ownership is still unproved.
- The route crosses multiple source owners: Auth/session/profile, profile/avatar Storage, Supabase Auth password/MFA, `preferences`, notifications, support tickets, billing/subscription, provider doctor profile, shell right panel, and global FAB/bottom action composition.
