Pass authority evidence:

- Pass 4 says own-user `/settings` is route-visible from `viewer` while its panel is admin-only, and it separates own-user settings from admin operations.
- Pass 4 blocks hard-coded/fallback role truth, identity-bearing browser logs, external avatar fallback identity disclosure, settings avatar URL logs, raw password-update Auth diagnostics, and corrupted `SecurityModal` password placeholders.
- Pass 4 allows direct Supabase Auth own-user operations only as a documented Auth adapter exception with MFA assurance, secret-display, loading, failed, and recovery states explicitly verified.
- Stage 5 classifies `/settings` provider professional profile edits as a Pass 5 self-service provider command surface and says operational availability/status, fee, and readiness cannot flow through unrestricted doctor-row updates.
- Pass 8 says `SettingsPage` renders an always-on notification/preference claim without a mounted preference receiver, static desktop `Free Tier`/`Upgrade` truth, and a Billing quick action that only toasts coming-soon text. Billing/subscription/preference truth must be a receiver-backed unavailable or proved state.
- Pass 8 also says `demo_mode_enabled` and patient privacy/consent lanes are not console operational settings unless explicitly product-approved and wired through an own-user operator preference projection.

Settings intake decisions:

- Data ownership: not admitted. Settings currently mixes AuthContext profile/session truth, display-id lookup, local DOM theme state, profile/avatar updates, Auth MFA/password operations, support route handoff, provider doctor writes, and static billing/security panel copy without one own-user settings projection.
- RBAC and shell: partially aligned, not admitted. The route, account nav, mobile settings entry, and `ContextPanel.jsx` now treat `/settings` as an own-user surface instead of admin-only. Future UI must still separate own-user settings from admin/system settings and prove right-panel actions, FAB, mobile action visibility, and receiver scope.
- Profile/avatar: blocked. Own-user profile editing and avatar upload may remain only after editable fields, Storage/media fallback policy, full reflected-read state, and failure feedback are proved. Raw profile/avatar browser diagnostics have a first redaction cleanup and the modal has focused upload/save pending-state proof, but the receiver and media policy are still unproved.
- Security/password/MFA: intake only. Direct Supabase Auth SDK operations are not admin authority. They require a documented own-user security controller or adapter exception, assurance-aware copy, pending/failed/recovery states, secret handling, and no raw Auth errors in browser diagnostics. Password-update browser diagnostics and raw MFA UI errors have first redaction cleanups, and the modal has focused pending-state proof, but the Auth adapter remains unproved.
- Preferences/notifications: blocked. The dark-mode toggle now uses the app `ThemeProvider` instead of page-owned DOM mutation, but notification preference wiring is unproved, and demo/privacy fields are not console settings truth. Future controls must persist through an own-user preference receiver or render unavailable.
- Billing/account plan: blocked. Static `Free Tier` and `Upgrade` are preserved as old inventory only; active desktop Settings now renders `Plan unavailable` / `Billing source not verified`, and the Settings panel Billing quick action gives unavailable status feedback until a billing receiver exists. The visual pass must use a user-scoped billing/subscription projection or keep the action unavailable with honest feedback.
- Provider profile: blocked. Provider doctor bio, fee, availability, and status writes belong to Pass 5 provider operations and cannot become Settings canon until a self-service field allowlist, role authority, reflected read, and app consequence are proved.
- Support shortcut: partially aligned. Active Settings no longer imports or renders private `SupportModal`; it routes eligible support actors to `/support-tickets?add=true&from=settings`, and non-support roles receive immediate unavailable feedback. The remaining blocker is that Settings itself is still not visually admitted.
- Global actions: partially quieted, not admitted. `ContextAwareFAB.jsx` and `DynamicBottomBar.jsx` now treat `/settings` as route-owned with no generic floating action, so unrelated global context actions do not appear. The first Settings visual pass must still choose one proved action model or keep actions unavailable.
- Hardgate: intentionally absent from the default set. `SettingsPage.jsx`, `MobileSettings.jsx`, and `SettingsPanel.jsx` have focused strict-radius proof for desktop shell/mobile shell/right-panel chrome, and `SecurityModal.jsx` plus `ProfileEditModal.jsx` have focused modal proof, but `SettingsPage.jsx`, `MobileSettings.jsx`, `SettingsPanel.jsx`, `SecurityModal.jsx`, `ProfileEditModal.jsx`, `SupportModal.jsx`, `DoctorProfileCard.jsx`, and `useDoctorProfile.js` stay out of `scripts/check-ui-surface-hardgate.js` until this blocker map is closed or the route enters a guarded implementation pass.

Required before Page 16 can move past intake:

1. Define one own-user settings projection for profile visibility, editable fields, avatar/media fallback, display id, role claims, preference readiness, notification readiness, and degraded states.
2. Replace the first shell alignment cleanup with a final Settings action model: route, navigation, context panel, right-panel actions, global FAB, mobile bottom action, and account menu visibility must distinguish own-user settings from admin/system settings.
3. Convert profile, security, support, and provider-detail modal patterns only after receiver ownership and submitted payloads are proved; shared chrome must not imply authority.
4. Prove the Auth password/MFA adapter: assurance level, challenge/enrollment, secret display, verification, unenroll, password update, loading, failure, retry, and redacted diagnostics.
5. Wire or disable notification and user preference controls through an own-user `preferences` receiver; keep the app-local theme control owned by `ThemeProvider`, and keep patient demo/consent fields outside Console settings unless explicitly approved.
6. Keep `Free Tier`, `Upgrade`, and Billing portal copy out of active Settings unless user-scoped billing/subscription truth is proved; otherwise keep a disabled/unavailable state with immediate feedback.
7. Move provider profile self-service to a Pass 5-owned field allowlist before availability/status/fee changes remain exposed.
8. Keep identity/media-bearing browser logs removed and repair visible settings/security mojibake when touching the affected files.
9. Add rendered desktop, tablet, and mobile proof plus hardgate coverage only when visual implementation starts.

Settings Requests-canon blocker map:

This is a blocker map, not a design target. Page 16 may not reuse Requests visual language until each slot below is proved, adapted, excluded, or marked legacy inactive from Settings' own old behavior.

| Requests canon slot | Settings current evidence | Page 16 admission decision |
|---|---|---|
| Signal field | The active page opens as a broad account/profile/preferences/security/billing/provider surface rather than one own-user settings signal. | Not admitted. Future signal must declare profile/security/preference/billing/provider readiness separately. |
| State choices | Desktop cards, mobile rows, right-panel quick actions, and query `?quick=true` open different action surfaces. | Not admitted. Future choices must map to one route-owned focus model with selected, pending, unavailable, denied, and failed states. |
| Handled sheet | Mobile has compact rows and desktop has cards, but neither is a shared handled sheet over one projection. | Not admitted. Future sheet must preserve mobile clarity while rendering the same own-user projection. |
| Focused detail | Private profile/security/support modals, provider card, provider doctor modal, and right panel all compete as detail surfaces. | Intake only. Detail must share modal/drawer rules and receiver ownership before reuse. |
| Route-owned action | Profile edit, security, support handoff, provider profile, sign out, Billing, and `?quick=true` can all be visible; Billing is unavailable with local feedback pending receiver proof, Support hands off to the admitted route owner, sign out now has local pending feedback and duplicate-tap blocking, and generic desktop/mobile floating actions are hidden on `/settings`. | Partially proved. Future pass must choose one receiver-backed action model and make unproved actions unavailable. |
| Shared modal/sheet | `ProfileEditModal` and `SecurityModal` use private chrome; Support handoff now uses the admitted Support route modal; provider detail mixes card and modal. | Intake only. Shared modal conversion waits for receiver proof. |
| Mobile recomposition | `MobileSettings` preserves useful Account/Preferences/Security rows and now has focused strict-radius/plain-label source proof for local mobile shell surfaces, but provider detail parity and preference/billing truth are unproved. | Not admitted. Mobile must stay simple while matching desktop authority. |
| Data quieting | Auth/profile/display-id reads, Auth SDK actions, support handoff, doctor update, context-panel events, and ThemeProvider-owned local theme state coexist. PageData startup and generic floating actions are quieted on `/settings`. | Partially proved. Future work must assign final read/action owners before `/settings` can become visual canon. |
| Local semantic color | Current page uses status/security/plan/provider labels without proved source state for all labels. | Not admitted. Future color must distinguish verified evidence, unavailable state, destructive/session actions, and neutral identity labels. |
| Interaction feedback | Sign out now acknowledges pending desktop/mobile state and blocks duplicate taps; `SecurityModal` has focused setup/verify/disable/password pending-state proof; `ProfileEditModal` has focused upload/save pending-state proof; support submit, theme toggle, provider update, rendered profile/avatar recovery states, and rendered security recovery states still need complete immediate feedback and reflected state proof. | Intake only. Future work must prove pressed, pending, disabled, denied, failed, stale, secret-handling, and receiver-refused states across every settings action. |

Promotion rule: the first Settings visual pass must close this blocker map before adding Page 16 to the default hardgate. Until then, Settings remains a Pass 4 identity/auth/settings intake surface with Pass 5 provider and Pass 8 shell dependencies, not a revamped Requests-style page.

### Page 17 Intake Audit - Subscriptions

Audit status: started on 2026-07-06; VISUAL-ONLY rollout pass applied on 2026-07-08. Decision: Subscriptions at `/subscriptions` has the VISUAL-ONLY Requests-canon energy applied to `MobileSubscriptions.jsx` (grounded status pill, subscription VitalTrack, identity-island detail, date-grouped feed, and a single read-only Details CTA), but remains command/backend intake only and is not admitted under the Today/Requests canon. The visual pass changed presentation only; no mutation was added. Command and backend authority stay unauthorized and fail-closed: subscriber edit/delete/status/type, welcome/custom/bulk email send, and export remain blocked pending backend authority and delivery-consequence proof, and no route-owned visual action promotion or default-hardgate promotion is authorized yet.

Why Subscriptions is Page 17:

- `/subscriptions` is an authenticated admin route behind `ProtectedRoute minRole="admin"`.
- It is visible in desktop Admin navigation as `Email Subscribers`; it has no role-primary mobile bottom-island entry.
- Pass 7 already classifies subscriber/email work as a sensitive marketing/contact lifecycle surface, not a page that can safely inherit Requests visual language from resemblance alone.
- The route crosses multiple source owners: `subscribers`, public acquisition/intake, `subscriptionService.js`, duplicate `subscribersService.js`, `useSubscription()`, welcome/custom/bulk Edge Function calls, campaign/unsubscribe templates, shell context panel, global FAB, mobile bottom action, and analytics consumers.

HEAD snapshot evidence for this ledger: `git show HEAD:frontend/src/components/pages/SubscriptionManagementPage.jsx`, `git show HEAD:frontend/src/components/mobile/MobileSubscriptions.jsx`, `git show HEAD:frontend/src/components/context/SubscriptionsPanel.jsx`, `git show HEAD:frontend/src/components/modals/SubscriptionModal.jsx`, `git show HEAD:frontend/src/components/views/SubscriptionListView.jsx`, `git show HEAD:frontend/src/components/views/SubscriptionTableView.jsx`, `git show HEAD:frontend/src/hooks/useSubscription.js`, `git show HEAD:frontend/src/services/subscriptionService.js`, and `git show HEAD:frontend/src/services/subscribersService.js` showed the old behavior inventory and current preservation targets: `usePageHeader('Subscription Management')`, `useViewMode('subscription', 'grid')`, `usePagination(20)`, `useSubscription()`, `openSubscriptionModal`, `openAnalyticsModal`, `ADD SUBSCRIBER`, desktop KPI cards for Total/Active/New/Paid/Free, local filter/search/pagination, grid/list/table renderers, `BulkActionBar`, single and bulk delete, create/update success toasts, `SubscriptionModal`, `AnalyticsModal`, `FilterSheet`, `ConfirmationModal`, `MobileSubscriptions`, mobile `Paid Conversion`, `Revenue Dynamics`, `LIVE` labels, row reveal/edit/delete, `SubscriptionsPanel` `Broadcast`, `Premium`, raw recent subscriber emails, modal welcome/custom/bulk email modes, campaign template loading, hook auto fetch/realtime, direct subscriber table writes, direct Edge Function invokes, and duplicate subscriber service exports.

Active source recertification:

- `App.js` mounts `/subscriptions` behind `ProtectedRoute minRole="admin"`, `routes.jsx` names it `resource: 'subscriptions'`, and desktop admin navigation labels it `Email Subscribers`.
- `mobileNavigation.js` has no primary bottom-navigation item for `/subscriptions`, so mobile access currently depends on overflow/account navigation rather than a role-primary island entry.
- `pageDataAccess.js` does not list a subscriber startup domain, and `/subscriptions` now has a zero-domain startup override. Current PageData startup is not the subscriber acquisition path; remaining subscriber truth still needs one named page projection owner.
- `SubscriptionManagementPage.jsx` owns the visible route with shell header/footer, local filtering, local counts, client-side pagination over the loaded rows, desktop grid/list/table variants, mobile recomposition, analytics modal input built from loaded rows, and a compact `subscriptionsRouteContextUpdated` projection for the right panel. First command safety cleanup on 2026-07-06 stopped the active page from destructuring `createSubscriber`, `updateSubscriber`, or `deleteSubscriber`; header add, context-panel Join, empty-state add, edit/delete buttons, mobile edit/delete, table selection, modal save, and bulk delete now fail closed or stay hidden with `Subscriber changes are not ready until subscriber authority is verified.` plus local `role="status"` feedback.
- `SubscriptionManagementPage.jsx` listens for `openSubscriptionModal` and `openAnalyticsModal`, but it does not listen for `openEmailActionsModal`.
- `SubscriptionsPanel.jsx` no longer dispatches `openEmailActionsModal`, no longer renders raw recent subscriber emails, no longer renders `Premium`, and labels the blocked email action as unavailable. It keeps `openSubscriptionModal` and `openAnalyticsModal` as route event bridges and consumes the page-published summary instead of a shell subscriber hook.
- `useSubscription.js` now accepts `autoFetch` and `autoSubscribe` options while preserving old page defaults. `ContextPanel.jsx` no longer mounts the subscriber hook, `ContextAwareFAB.jsx` and `DynamicBottomBar.jsx` hide generic action chrome on `/subscriptions`, and `Analytics.jsx` no longer imports or mounts `useSubscription`, so hidden shell/analytics mounts no longer fetch subscribers or open broad realtime channels before a proved route/action owns subscriber truth.
- The default Subscriptions page hook still auto-fetches subscribers and creates a broad realtime subscription. It also exposes create, update, delete, status, type, mark-welcome, analytics, and welcome-send commands, so the page remains intake-only until projection and receiver authority are assigned.
- `subscriptionService.js` is the active workflow facade, but it directly reads and writes `subscribers`, returns `[]` for unauthorized or failed admin list reads, calculates analytics locally from loaded rows, invokes `sendWelcome`, `sendCustomEmail`, and `sendBulkEmail`, and owns broad realtime channels.
- `subscribersService.js` remains a duplicate compatibility table adapter for subscriber CRUD/count/realtime and must not become a second active owner.
- `SubscriptionModal.jsx` uses private modal chrome, can run create/edit/view/bulk/email-actions modes, imports email functions directly, loads active bulk recipients, can send welcome/custom/bulk email, loads the iVisit 1.0.6 campaign template, and shows campaign/broadcast copy before email lifecycle proof is closed.
- HEAD mobile inventory derived `Paid Conversion`, `Revenue Dynamics`, `Monetization`, `Paid Mix`, `LIVE`, and hard-coded chart data from `subscribers.type` and loaded rows. Active mobile no longer renders those revenue/live labels after the cleanup below, but the remaining loaded-row counts and hard-coded chart shapes are still subscriber tier/mix inventory, not payment, revenue, entitlement, or billing proof.
- Subscriptions mobile/right-panel squircle and source-voice cleanup on 2026-07-06 converted `MobileSubscriptions.jsx` and `SubscriptionsPanel.jsx` to semantic radius tokens, removed local decorative border/ring/outline/tracking/all-caps mobile chrome, replaced loaded-row `LIVE`/revenue/monetization wording with loaded-row subscriber mix language, and preserved search, filters, analytics trigger, row reveal, pull-to-refresh, load more, route-summary panel counts, Join/Data event bridges, disabled Email off, and unavailable command guards. This gives `MobileSubscriptions.jsx` and `SubscriptionsPanel.jsx` focused strict-radius and source-voice proof only; it does not admit Subscriptions, prove subscriber projection/counts, prove email delivery, or make loaded-row charts canonical.
- Subscriptions VISUAL-ONLY rollout pass on 2026-07-08 applied the Requests-canon energy to `MobileSubscriptions.jsx`: the row renders a grounded status pill from `resolveVital('subscription', ...)`, a subscription VitalTrack (pending to active; cancelled/expired/inactive muted) in the expanded detail, `MobileDetailIslands` identity tiles (email, plan/type, status, subscribed date, welcome email), a `groupByMonth` date-grouped feed, plan/type meta on the row secondary line, and a single read-only `MobileSheetActions` Details CTA; the status-only rightBlade was removed. This pass changed presentation only; it added no mutation, kept `onEdit`/`onDelete` unwired with `canManage={false}`, and did not grant subscriber command/backend authority, prove subscriber projection/counts, prove email delivery, or admit Page 17 to the default hardgate.

Pass authority evidence:

- The Pass 7 subscription subplan says it is a detailed implementation subplan only and authorizes no product, database, Edge Function, cleanup, email send, seed, migration, or runtime mutation.
- The Pass 7 evidence audit says subscription management has too many owners for one lifecycle, exposes management writes not authorized by current source RLS, and renders unsupported commercial meaning from a marketing subscriber row.
- The Pass 7 evidence audit says plain `createSubscriber()` is row-only, optional welcome is explicit, and the remaining high-risk defect is that `sendWelcome` can deliver mail while leaving `welcome_email_sent` false, allowing the batch worker to redeliver.
- The Pass 7 evidence audit says current RLS evidence authorizes public insert and admin select, not rendered update/delete/status/type commands.
- The Pass 7 checklist says subscriber edit/delete/status/type/unsubscribe/export and welcome/custom/bulk email send are excluded from the first implementation slice.
- The Pass 7 checklist says to stop hidden shell controls from mounting full protected subscriber reads, disable false bulk deletes and dead broadcast events, keep email lifecycle commands unavailable until deployed slug/idempotency/durable state/per-recipient results are proved, and never treat `subscribers.type = paid` as payment, revenue, entitlement, or billing proof.
- Stage 5 classifies `subscriptionService.js` as the active Pass 7 subscription/email lifecycle surface and `subscribersService.js` as duplicate-risk compatibility scope.

Subscriptions intake decisions:

- Data ownership: not admitted. The active page hook, active service, duplicate service, and explicit modal/email actions can still touch subscriber truth. Hidden shell hooks are quiet and Analytics no longer mounts the subscriber hook, but a future pass must still name one bounded admin subscriber projection owner for rows, exact count, search, filters, sorting, pagination, stale refresh, failure, and read-state labels.
- RBAC and scope: not admitted. The route is admin-only and current source treats subscribers as a platform-admin global marketing list. No org-admin, provider, sponsor, patient, organization, or facility scope may be inferred from this table.
- PageData/shell ownership: partially quieted, not admitted. `/subscriptions` now has a zero-domain startup override, ContextPanel requests the page-published `subscriptionsRouteContextUpdated` projection instead of mounting a subscriber hook, and generic desktop/mobile action chrome is hidden on the route. The visible page still needs a proved projection owner.
- Command authority: blocked and fail-closed in active UI. Edit, delete, bulk delete, status, type, mark-welcome, export, and unsubscribe commands remain old behavior inventory until a lifecycle receiver, actor scope, RLS/RPC/Edge expectation, audit/reflected-read path, and app/public consequence are proved. Active page edit/delete/select/bulk paths now show unavailable feedback or stay hidden instead of calling subscriber mutations.
- Create authority: intake only and fail-closed in active UI. Row-only create is the closest current policy-backed action, but the old visible create path could default `sendWelcomeEmail` through modal/hook behavior. Active header/context/empty-state create now shows unavailable feedback until create-without-email is separated from any welcome command.
- Email lifecycle: blocked. Welcome, custom, bulk, campaign, unsubscribe, and bulk-recipient actions must remain unavailable until deployed slugs, idempotency, durable `welcome_email_sent`/unsubscribe state, per-recipient result visibility, privacy rules, and non-production test paths are proved. The right-panel Broadcast action is disabled as `Email off` until that receiver proof exists.
- Paid/billing semantics: blocked. `type = paid`, old `Paid Conversion`, old `Revenue Dynamics`, and old `Monetization` remain marketing tier inventory only unless a billing/payment/subscription outcome projection is joined. The right panel no longer renders `Premium`; it labels the count as `Paid type`. Mobile no longer renders `Paid Conversion`, `Revenue Dynamics`, `Monetization`, `LIVE`, or all-caps paid/free blades in active source.
- Shared modal/panel: intake only. `SubscriptionModal` uses private chrome and direct command imports; `SubscriptionsPanel` now uses the route summary, hides raw email rows, and disables the dead Broadcast command. Shared chrome or visual polish cannot promote the remaining modal commands.
- Mobile recomposition: not admitted. Mobile preserves useful compact rows, pull-to-refresh, search, filters, analytics trigger, row reveal, and load-more inventory. Active mobile now labels loaded-row counts as shown/loaded subscriber mix instead of live revenue or payment truth, but it still needs a server projection, exact counts, and non-placeholder trend/chart source before it can become canonical.
- Hardgate: intentionally absent. `MobileSubscriptions.jsx` and `SubscriptionsPanel.jsx` have focused strict-radius/source-voice proof, but `SubscriptionManagementPage.jsx`, `MobileSubscriptions.jsx`, `SubscriptionsPanel.jsx`, `SubscriptionModal.jsx`, `SubscriptionListView.jsx`, `SubscriptionTableView.jsx`, `useSubscription.js`, `subscriptionService.js`, and `subscribersService.js` stay out of `scripts/check-ui-surface-hardgate.js` until this blocker map is closed or the route enters a guarded implementation pass.

Required before Page 17 can move past intake:

1. Define one subscriber projection owner for admin-scoped rows, exact count, search, filters, sort, pagination, stale refresh, failed/denied/unavailable states, and realtime invalidation.
2. Remove hidden subscriber acquisition from global shell/action containers or make it action-owned and lazy behind route/auth/capability gates.
3. Consolidate `subscriptionService.js` as the only active facade and keep `subscribersService.js` compatibility-only until deletion proof exists.
4. Build one capability map for create, create-with-welcome, edit, delete, bulk delete, status, type, welcome, custom email, bulk email, export, and unsubscribe; disabled/unavailable states must explain missing receiver or scope where useful.
5. Separate row-only create from welcome delivery; keep email send controls unavailable until deployed receiver slugs, idempotency, durable state, per-recipient results, unsubscribe topology, and redacted diagnostics are proved.
6. Replace remaining loaded-row totals and mobile charts with server aggregates, exact counts, or unavailable/source-pending states. The active mobile copy now says shown/loaded, but the data source is still not canonical.
7. Keep paid/revenue/premium/payment wording out of active subscriber UI unless a billing/payment owner is joined. Old paid/revenue labels remain inventory only.
8. Choose one action model for the route: page header, right panel, mobile route action, or global action. `/subscriptions` cannot keep competing create/broadcast entry points.
9. Convert modal/panel chrome only after receiver ownership and submitted payloads are truthful; shared chrome must not imply command authority.
10. Repair visible subscriber mojibake when touching `SubscriptionsPanel.jsx` and run the encoding gate on touched files.
11. Add rendered desktop, tablet, and mobile proof plus hardgate coverage only when visual implementation starts.

### Page 18 Intake Audit - Pricing

Audit status: started on 2026-07-06. Decision: Pricing at `/pricing` is intake only and is not admitted under the Today/Requests canon. No visual revamp, shared Requests pattern reuse, price create/edit/delete/bulk delete, org-wide pricing save, context-panel command promotion, route-owned action promotion, or hardgate promotion is authorized yet.

Why Pricing is Page 18:

- `/pricing` is an authenticated route behind `ProtectedRoute minRole="org_admin"`.
- It is visible in desktop Payments navigation as `Pricing`; it has no role-primary mobile bottom-island entry.
- Pass 3 classifies pricing with hospitals, capacity, provider discovery, and facility scope. Pricing is not a decorative finance table.
- The route crosses multiple source owners: `service_pricing`, `room_pricing`, pricing RPCs, hospital/facility identity, organization identity, `PricingManagementPage.jsx`, `MobilePricing.jsx`, `PricingContextPanel.jsx`, `PageDataContext`, global FAB/mobile bottom action wiring, and the patient app billing quote path.

HEAD snapshot evidence for this ledger: `git show HEAD:frontend/src/components/pages/PricingManagementPage.jsx`, `git show HEAD:frontend/src/components/mobile/MobilePricing.jsx`, `git show HEAD:frontend/src/components/context/PricingContextPanel.jsx`, `git show HEAD:frontend/src/components/views/PricingTableView.jsx`, `git show HEAD:frontend/src/components/views/PricingListView.jsx`, and `git show HEAD:frontend/src/services/pricingService.js` showed the old behavior inventory: `usePageHeader('Pricing Engine')`, `useViewMode('pricing', 'grid')`, `usePagination(12)`, active tabs for All/Services/Rooms, local KPI filter for all/global/override, local search/filter/pagination over loaded rows, desktop KPI cards for average/global/override values, `PricingTableView`, `PricingListView`, `MobilePricing`, `BulkActionBar`, `ConfirmationModal`, `AnalyticsModal`, private pricing modal chrome, `openPricingModal`, single and bulk delete, `saveServicePricing`, `saveRoomPricing`, `deleteServicePricing`, `deleteRoomPricing`, `parseFloat(formData.price)`, success/error toasts, mobile `Average Service Price`, `Average Room Price`, `Pricing Dynamics`, `Avg Price`, `LIVE` labels, row reveal/edit/delete, context-panel average/global/override counts, `getPricing()` broad hospital joins, first-hospital organization write resolution, and upsert/delete RPC payloads.

Active source recertification:

- `App.js` mounts `/pricing` behind `ProtectedRoute minRole="org_admin"`, `routes.jsx` names it `resource: 'pricing'`, and desktop Payments navigation labels it `Pricing`.
- `mobileNavigation.js` has no primary bottom-navigation item for `/pricing`, so mobile access currently depends on overflow/account navigation rather than a role-primary island entry.
- First route startup quieting cleanup on 2026-07-06 made `/pricing` route-owned in `pageDataAccess.js` with a zero-domain startup override. `PageDataContext.jsx` no longer starts broad `servicePricing`/`roomPricing` reads or `pricing_changes` realtime while the Pricing route is open.
- First right-panel truth cleanup on 2026-07-06 stopped `ContextPanel.jsx` from passing `servicePricing` and `roomPricing` into `/pricing`. `PricingContextPanel.jsx` now renders source-pending facility/room/quote signals and unavailable action feedback instead of blended averages or loaded-row scope counts.
- `PricingManagementPage.jsx` owns the visible route with shell header/footer, `getPricingPageData()` route projection reads, projection-backed search/scope/pagination/counts, desktop grid/list/table style surfaces, mobile recomposition, analytics modal input built from the projection summary, and unavailable create/update/delete feedback while mutation authority is unproved.
- `PricingManagementPage.jsx` still listens for `openPricingModal`, but the listener now only shows unavailable feedback. The active page no longer imports pricing mutation functions and no longer carries the private price edit modal; the old modal and direct command paths remain HEAD/service inventory only.
- First shell action quieting cleanup on 2026-07-06 made `ContextAwareFAB.jsx` and `DynamicBottomBar.jsx` treat `/pricing` as route-owned, so the generic desktop/mobile `Add price` context action no longer mounts on the Pricing route.
- Active command safety cleanup on 2026-07-06 now keeps `PricingManagementPage.jsx` fail-closed for price mutations without importing or calling pricing mutation functions. Header add, `openPricingModal`, empty-state create, row view/edit/delete, and mobile row actions now show unavailable feedback; table selection and bulk delete stay hidden while `PRICING_MUTATION_COMMANDS_ENABLED` is false. The old private modal and save/delete functions remain HEAD/service inventory only.
- `pricingService.js` now exposes `getPricingPageData()` as the active route read projection, with source-labelled rows, facility names, USD amount normalization, current-filter summary values, and failed-read propagation. It still reads all hospitals plus all price rows before filtering in service code, so this is not yet the final server-backed pricing projection.
- Legacy `getPricing()` still reads all hospitals plus all price rows, maps `hospital_id` back to `organization_id`, filters in memory for org scope, and remains source inventory for older consumers.
- `pricingService.js` writes through `upsert_service_pricing`, `upsert_room_pricing`, `delete_service_pricing`, and `delete_room_pricing`, but the UI does not collect an explicit facility id for organization-level copy.
- `PricingTableView.jsx` and `PricingListView.jsx` render currency from raw row amount fields, expose view/edit/delete when `canEdit(item)` passes, and preserve old density inventory.
- `MobilePricing.jsx` now consumes the route projection summary for total, platform fallback, facility price, and average amount labels. The old loaded-row time-window trend and `LIVE` labels remain HEAD evidence only; active mobile renders the current projection basis instead of live/trend claims.
- Pricing mobile/right-panel squircle and source-voice cleanup on 2026-07-06 converted `MobilePricing.jsx` and `PricingContextPanel.jsx` to semantic radius tokens, removed local decorative border/ring/outline/tracking/all-caps mobile chrome, changed `Pricing Dynamics` and `Avg Price` to source-neutral active labels, and preserved search, tabs, analytics trigger, row reveal, unavailable action notice, projection-basis metrics, source-pending panel signals, and fail-closed panel actions. This gives `MobilePricing.jsx` and `PricingContextPanel.jsx` focused strict-radius and source-voice proof only; it does not admit Pricing, prove selected-facility projection truth, prove price mutation receivers, or prove patient app quote consequence.
- `ivisit-app/services/billingQuoteService.js` calls `get_billing_quote`, and `ivisit-app/utils/billingQuotePresentation.js` applies returned quote amounts to the displayed cost. Console pricing changes must therefore prove selected-hospital app quote consequence before active writes are trusted.

Pass authority evidence:

- Stage 4 classifies Pricing as `service_pricing`, `room_pricing`, pricing RPCs, and selected hospital quote resolution.
- Stage 6 Pass 3 lists `PricingManagementPage.jsx`, `PricingTableView.jsx`, `MobilePricing.jsx`, `pricingService.js`, and `ivisit-app/services/pricingService.js` in the hospital, capacity, discovery, and pricing scope.
- The Pass 3 subplan says `pricingService` maps hospital-scoped pricing back to organization scope and chooses the first hospital for writes.
- The Pass 3 subplan says `/pricing` renders service/room rows and summary values from unwindowed/broad pricing reads.
- The Pass 3 subplan says `MobilePricing` calculates average price, global/override counts, and time-window trends from supplied rows.
- The Pass 3 subplan says Pricing is hospital/facility-scoped for active patient quotes, with `service_pricing` for facility service types and `room_pricing` for facility room/bed categories.
- The Pass 3 subplan says null-hospital rows must be explicit platform defaults/fallbacks and must not be presented as organization-managed pricing.
- The Pass 3 subplan says organization-level presentation is aggregation only unless a future propagation receiver deliberately owns it.
- The Pass 3 subplan says multi-hospital organizations cannot silently write pricing to the first hospital.
- The Pass 3 subplan says app quote comparison for selected hospital pricing is required after implementation.
- The Pass 3 subplan says not to implement pricing writes without explicit facility selection and never to write an implicit earliest-hospital price.

Pricing intake decisions:

- Data ownership: partially improved, not admitted. The first safety cleanup removed PageData startup acquisition, route-level pricing realtime, right-panel PageData pricing arrays, and generic FAB/mobile `Add price` action on `/pricing`. The active page now consumes `getPricingPageData()` for rows, source labels, current-filter counts, search, scope, and pagination. It is still not admitted because that projection is service-side broad-read/current-filter logic, not a server-backed exact projection with stale refresh, denied/unavailable states, realtime invalidation, and app quote consequence.
- Scope: not admitted. Current copy and form flow blur global, organization, hospital, service, and room scope. Future UI must render explicit facility identity or keep facility-scoped writes unavailable.
- Command authority: blocked. Active command safety cleanup moved create, edit, delete, selection, and bulk delete paths to unavailable feedback, hidden state, or active-source removal while keeping the old private modal/RPC behavior as HEAD/service inventory only. These commands remain unavailable until RPC/RLS role authority, explicit facility payload fields, reflected read, audit/failure state, and app quote consequence are proved.
- Organization-wide pricing: blocked. The current receiver is hospital-scoped, and there is no proved propagation receiver or sibling-hospital conflict model.
- PageData/shell ownership: partially quieted, not admitted. `/pricing` is now route-owned in `pageDataAccess.js`, and the right panel no longer consumes PageData pricing rows. This does not define the pricing projection owner or prove app quote consequence.
- Route-owned action: partially fail-closed, not admitted. Generic desktop/mobile `Add price`, right-panel pricing dispatches, page header `Add Pricing`, row view/edit/delete, mobile view/edit/delete, table selection, and bulk delete are hidden, removed, or unavailable while mutation authority is unproved. A future pass still needs one capability map and receiver proof before any visual pass.
- Metrics and reports: partially improved, not admitted. Desktop/mobile counts, average amount, and analytics values now come from the route projection summary, and mobile no longer renders `LIVE` trend labels for active pricing metrics. They remain current-filter values from broad service reads, not selected-hospital quote proof or server aggregate truth.
- Shared modal/panel: intake only. The old pricing create/edit modal used private chrome and is now absent from the active page while command authority is unproved. Shared chrome or animation polish cannot promote unproved price mutation authority.
- Mobile recomposition: not admitted. Mobile preserves useful compact search, tabs, metrics, pull-to-refresh, row reveal, and edit/delete inventory. Active mobile now uses projection summary labels, current-basis copy, semantic radius tokens, and plain source labels, but it still needs final projection, receiver proof, and selected-hospital quote consequence before admission.
- Hardgate: intentionally absent. `MobilePricing.jsx` and `PricingContextPanel.jsx` have focused strict-radius/source-voice proof, but `PricingManagementPage.jsx`, `MobilePricing.jsx`, `PricingContextPanel.jsx`, `PricingTableView.jsx`, `PricingListView.jsx`, and `pricingService.js` stay out of `scripts/check-ui-surface-hardgate.js` until this blocker map is closed or the route enters a guarded implementation pass.

Required before Page 18 can move past intake:

1. Define one pricing projection owner for facility-scoped service and room rows, exact count, search, filters, sort, pagination, stale refresh, failed/denied/unavailable states, realtime invalidation, and source labels.
2. Complete the route/PageData boundary: keep `/pricing` route-owned, feed any future right panel from the route projection, and prove no duplicate startup truth returns.
3. Prove `service_pricing` and `room_pricing` schema/RLS/RPC contracts, actor role scope, submitted payload fields, reflected read, and failure model.
4. Resolve multi-hospital organization pricing with explicit facility selection or disable org-admin pricing save until a propagation/conflict receiver exists.
5. Prove app quote consequence by comparing selected-hospital pricing against the patient app quote path.
6. Keep create/edit/delete/bulk delete unavailable or disabled until receiver authority and app consequence are closed.
7. Replace interim current-filter summary values with server aggregate, selected-facility, or unavailable/source-pending labels before admission.
8. Choose one action model for the route: page header, right panel, route-owned mobile action, or global action. `/pricing` cannot keep competing create entry points.
9. Convert modal/panel chrome only after receiver ownership and submitted payloads are truthful; shared chrome must not imply command authority.
10. Add rendered desktop, tablet, and mobile proof plus hardgate coverage only when visual implementation starts.

### Page 19 Admission - Login

Audit status: intake started 2026-07-06; the Login public-auth visual surface is admitted for guarded continuation on 2026-07-07 after its first full visual pass and a signed-out desktop/mobile-safety rendered proof of the email step. Decision: `LoginPage.jsx` at `/login` is admitted under the Today/Requests canon as a public auth-gateway surface, visual surface only. Only the visual surface is admitted. The auth receivers stay excluded: Supabase Auth password sign-in, the `check-user` Edge Function, MFA list/challenge/verify, Google OAuth, and password-reset links remain intake-blocked until their receiver, deployment, redirect, and deep-link authority proofs close. No auth-flow rewrite, auth copy rewrite, OAuth/reset/MFA promotion, or Requests pattern reuse is authorized. The email step must not be submitted from a proof session because `check-user` is a live Edge call and the no-password path sends a real reset email.

Why Login is Page 19:

- `/login` is a public route mounted outside `ProtectedRoute`.
- `routes.jsx` names `/login` as `public: true`, while `ProtectedRoute.jsx` redirects unauthenticated protected-route access to `/login`.
- `AppShell` hides the authenticated console shell, shared nav, right context panel, FAB, bottom bar, and startup overlay for `/login`; this is a public auth-shell exception, not a reusable console page layout.
- The route crosses multiple authority owners: Supabase Auth password sign-in, Supabase MFA list/challenge/verify, Supabase OAuth, Supabase password reset links, the `check-user` Edge Function, AuthProvider session/profile redirects, onboarding handoff, set-password recovery, and external support handoff.

HEAD snapshot evidence for this ledger: `git show HEAD:frontend/src/components/pages/LoginPage.jsx`, `git show HEAD:frontend/src/App.js`, `git show HEAD:frontend/src/config/routes.jsx`, `git show HEAD:frontend/src/components/common/ProtectedRoute.jsx`, and `git show HEAD:frontend/src/contexts/AuthContext.jsx` showed the old/current behavior inventory: email-first progressive step, `check-user` Edge Function, email validation, account-not-found feedback, optional password setup reset link, password sign-in through `signIn(email, password)`, MFA factor listing, MFA challenge and verify, Google OAuth, forgot-password reset link to `/set-password`, organization registration link to `/onboarding`, external support link, auth redirect to `/`, shell-hidden public route handling, and unauthenticated protected-route redirect to `/login`.

- Targeted recent history check: `git log --since='2026-06-28' -- frontend/src/components/pages/LoginPage.jsx frontend/src/App.js frontend/src/config/routes.jsx frontend/src/contexts/AuthContext.jsx frontend/src/services/authService.js frontend/src/lib/supabase.js frontend/src/utils/errorHandler.js` returned no newer committed changes for those anchor files. The Login preservation baseline for this intake pass remains committed `HEAD` at `f31f29f`; active Login work is uncommitted source on top of that baseline.
- Active source recertification: `LoginPage.jsx` still owns the visible public route, progressive email/password/2FA steps, `check-user`, OAuth, reset-password, onboarding, support, toast/error, and theme-toggle behaviors. `App.js` still mounts `/login` without `ProtectedRoute` and includes `/login` in `hideNav`. `routes.jsx` still marks `/login` public. `ProtectedRoute.jsx` still redirects unauthenticated protected-route access to `/login` and includes `/login` in the allowed public path list. `AuthContext.jsx` still performs the actual password sign-in through `supabase.auth.signInWithPassword`.
- First MFA receiver cleanup on 2026-07-06 separated `mfaFactorId` from `mfaChallengeId` in `LoginPage.jsx`. The login MFA challenge now stores `factor.id` as the factor id and `challenge.id` as the challenge id, then submits both to `supabase.auth.mfa.verify({ factorId, challengeId, code })`. The old code sent the challenge id as both values.
- Second MFA state cleanup on 2026-07-06 clears stale MFA state before each password sign-in, validates the local six-digit code before receiver calls, and sends users back to password sign-in with visible feedback when the challenge state is missing or expired.
- Login public-auth squircle/source-feedback cleanup on 2026-07-06 converted `LoginPage.jsx` local public-auth chrome to semantic radius tokens (`rounded-icon`, `rounded-inner`, `rounded-button`, `rounded-pill`), removed decorative border/ring/outline/tracking/all-caps chrome, removed unproved security/status display copy (`Mission-critical emergency response coordination link.`, `System Online`, `Encrypted`, and `SECURE CONNECTION`), removed public-auth browser debug logging, and preserved email/password/2FA progression, `check-user`, OAuth, reset-password, onboarding/support links, theme toggle, and MFA factor/challenge cleanup. This is focused strict-radius/source-voice proof only; it does not admit Login, prove auth receivers, prove public route rendered mobile proof, or add Login to the default hardgate.
- First Login visual pass on 2026-07-07 admitted the Login visual surface and moved it off brand red. The 2026-07-06 cleanup already had canonical radius; this pass removed the remaining decorative and red chrome across all three steps and the branding panel: `backdrop-blur-sm` glass status pills, `focus-within:shadow-xl focus-within:shadow-primary/5` input glows, the `animate-pulse` status dot, and the `glass-card` theme-toggle container. It neutralized every red accent — `bg-primary`/`hover:bg-primary/90`/`text-primary-foreground`/`shadow-primary/20` submit buttons became neutral `bg-foreground`/`text-background`; the `text-primary` brand period, shield icons, `bg-primary/10` shield tiles, input focus icons, `Server` icon, and the red `Register your organization`/`Forgot your password?` links became `foreground`/`muted` (links kept a persistent underline) — because `--primary` resolves to `rgb(115, 17, 22)` in this theme and none of these were danger/destructive states. It preserved every auth path: the email/password/2FA progression, `check-user`, `emailSchema` validation, account-not-found feedback, the no-password reset nudge, `signIn`, MFA list/challenge/verify with the fixed factor/challenge state, Google OAuth, forgot-password reset to `/set-password`, the `/onboarding` register link, the external support link, and the theme toggle. Legitimate non-red status color was kept: emerald identity/confirmation markers and destructive error feedback. `LoginPage.jsx` is now in the default UI hardgate and passes `npm run check:ui-hardgate:revamp -- src/components/pages/LoginPage.jsx`.
- Login admission scope guard: this admission is visual surface only. It does not certify or admit any auth receiver, does not prove the `check-user` Edge Function contract, does not prove Supabase Auth/OAuth/MFA/reset payloads or redirect outcomes, does not prove app-consequence, and does not authorize a Requests multi-data pattern. The still-blocked receiver decisions below remain in force.
- Rendered proof, 2026-07-07: signed out through the app's own Settings sign-out (which revokes the session and clears it across tabs), then drove the running `localhost:3000` dev server as an anonymous visitor. `/login?proof=login-signedout-2026-07-07` rendered the email step for the signed-out path without redirecting to `/`. A scoped DOM audit (excluding fixed global overlays) reported zero elements on the login surface computing to `rgb(115, 17, 22)` or `rgb(160, 24, 31)`, and the `Continue` submit button computed to a neutral `rgb(255, 255, 255)` (formerly a red `bg-primary` button); horizontal overflow was zero. The only page reds were global overlays outside Page 19 scope (the theme-toggle glow and the PWA/dev pill). The branding panel showed a muted shield tile and a muted `iVisit.` period, the status pills were calm (no glass, no pulse), and the `Register your organization` link rendered as a neutral underlined link. The standalone public shell showed no authenticated console nav, no bottom bar, and no startup overlay, and the browser console reported no errors or warnings. The password and 2FA steps were certified statically by the strict-radius hardgate and the residual-token grep (they share the identical neutralized button/input classes) rather than driven, to avoid the live `check-user` Edge call and the no-password reset email.

Login intake decisions:

- Auth receiver ownership: partially proved, not admitted. Password sign-in delegates to `AuthContext.signIn()`, which calls Supabase Auth. The page still calls `check-user`, OAuth, reset-password, and MFA APIs directly, so a future pass must classify which calls stay page-owned and which move behind an auth service adapter.
- Edge Function truth: not admitted. `check-user` may improve UX, but the deployed function owner, denied/error semantics, timeout behavior, rate limit feedback, and account-enumeration risk need proof before visual copy or flow sequencing is promoted.
- MFA: safer, not admitted. The first cleanup fixes factor/challenge state, and the second cleanup prevents malformed or missing challenge state from reaching the receiver. The route still needs fixture or rendered proof for verified factors, challenge failures, expired challenges, wrong code, retry, and user-switch cleanup before the 2FA surface is treated as complete.
- OAuth and reset links: not admitted. Google OAuth, password setup, forgot-password, `/set-password`, and production redirect origins need public-flow proof before any visual rewrite.
- Onboarding handoff: not admitted. `/login` links to `/onboarding`, but onboarding is a separate public shell route and must get its own intake before Login copy can promise organization registration outcomes.
- Shell/layout: public-shell exception only. Login may hide the authenticated console frame, but it cannot define a second global shell pattern for console pages.
- Data/privacy copy: not admitted. The active source no longer renders the old security/connection/status claims, but public-auth copy, third-party icon/media choices, account-enumeration posture, and support/onboarding claims still need review before admission.
- Hardgate: visual surface admitted 2026-07-07. `LoginPage.jsx` is now in the default `scripts/check-ui-surface-hardgate.js` surface. The auth-authority files (`AuthContext.jsx`, `authService.js`, the `check-user` Edge Function) stay out of the hardgate until the auth receivers are proved and admitted. Strict-radius proof for the surface may be run with `npm run check:ui-hardgate:revamp -- src/components/pages/LoginPage.jsx`.

Required before Page 19 can move past intake:

1. Define one auth flow owner for email existence, password sign-in, OAuth, MFA, reset-password, onboarding handoff, session redirects, denied/error states, loading states, and recovery states.
2. Prove the deployed `check-user` Edge Function contract, failure behavior, privacy posture, and whether it may reveal account existence.
3. Prove Supabase Auth receiver payloads and outcomes for password sign-in, OAuth redirect, password reset, and MFA verification.
4. Prove `/login`, `/set-password`, `/onboarding`, and `/onboarding-success` redirect/deep-link behavior together before changing public auth copy or flow order.
5. Keep unproved security claims, fragile direct-provider media, browser debug logging, and generic command copy out of the active public-auth surface; any future copy change must stay simple and receiver-backed.
6. Add desktop, tablet, and mobile rendered proof with no authenticated-shell leakage, no startup overlay, no duplicate nav, and immediate feedback on every submit/toggle/link.
7. Add hardgate coverage only after the public auth visual system is converted to shared tokens, squircle geometry, structural loading/error states, and intentional motion.

Login Requests-canon blocker map:

This is a blocker map, not a design target. Page 19 may not reuse Requests visual language because Login is a public auth gateway, not a multi-data work stage.

| Focused detail | Password reset, OAuth, onboarding, support, and 2FA are secondary paths. | Intake only. Each secondary path needs receiver/deep-link proof before visual promotion. |

Future proof must cover every click/tap/submit, including receiver errors and route transitions. |

The auth-authority files (`AuthContext.jsx`, `authService.js`, the `check-user` Edge Function) remain out of the default hardgate, and Login may not reuse Requests multi-data visual language until its receiver, redirect, deep-link, and app-consequence blockers close.

### Page 20 Admission - Set Password

Audit status: intake started 2026-07-06; the Set Password public-recovery visual surface is admitted for guarded continuation on 2026-07-07 after its first full visual pass and a signed-out desktop/mobile-safety rendered proof of the recovery-checking/missing state. Decision: `SetPasswordPage.jsx` at `/set-password` is admitted under the Today/Requests canon as a public recovery-gateway surface, visual surface only. Only the visual surface is admitted. The recovery receivers stay excluded: recovery-session parsing, `supabase.auth.updateUser({ password })`, and the post-update redirect remain intake-blocked until their session, receiver, and deep-link authority proofs close. No recovery-flow rewrite, recovery copy rewrite, Auth update promotion, or Requests pattern reuse is authorized. The form must not be submitted from a proof session because submit performs a live `updateUser` password write.

Why Set Password is Page 20:

- `/set-password` is a public route mounted outside `ProtectedRoute`.
- `routes.jsx` names `/set-password` as `public: true`, and `ProtectedRoute.jsx` includes it in the public allowed path list.
- `AppShell` hides the authenticated console shell, shared nav, right context panel, FAB, bottom bar, and startup overlay for `/set-password`; this is a public recovery-shell exception, not a reusable console page layout.
- `AuthContext.jsx` redirects Supabase `PASSWORD_RECOVERY` auth events to `/set-password`, and Login reset links use `redirectTo: ${window.location.origin}/set-password`.
- The route crosses multiple authority owners: Supabase Auth recovery session parsing, AuthProvider recovery redirects, `supabase.auth.getSession()`, `supabase.auth.updateUser({ password })`, password validation, Login reset links, route redirect to `/`, and invalid/expired-link feedback.

- Recovery-session truth: safer, not admitted. - Redirect behavior: safer, not admitted. Success still waits one second then navigates to `/`, now with route-unmount cleanup; future proof must verify whether recovered users with pending onboarding should go to Today, onboarding, or a dedicated success state.
- Login handoff: not admitted. Login sends setup/reset links to `/set-password`, but Login and Set Password need end-to-end proof before public auth copy changes.

- Data/privacy copy: not admitted. The active source no longer renders the old security/handshake claim, but public-auth recovery copy, password policy copy, account recovery privacy posture, and post-update destination still need review before admission.
- Hardgate: visual surface admitted 2026-07-07. `SetPasswordPage.jsx` is now in the default `scripts/check-ui-surface-hardgate.js` surface. The recovery-authority owners (the `AuthContext.jsx` `PASSWORD_RECOVERY` redirect and `supabase.auth.updateUser`) stay out of the hardgate until the recovery receivers are proved and admitted. Strict-radius proof may be run with `npm run check:ui-hardgate:revamp -- src/components/pages/SetPasswordPage.jsx`.

2. Prove Supabase Auth recovery-session behavior for links from Login, email, browser refresh, expired link, and already-used link.

4. Prove `/login`, `/set-password`, `/onboarding`, and `/onboarding-success` redirect/deep-link behavior together before changing public auth copy or flow order.
5. Keep unproved security claims, fragile public recovery copy, browser debug logging, and generic command copy out of the active recovery surface; any future copy change must stay simple and receiver-backed.
6. Add desktop, tablet, and mobile rendered proof with no authenticated-shell leakage, no startup overlay, no duplicate nav, visible checking/missing-link state, and immediate feedback on every submit/toggle/link.
7. Add hardgate coverage only after the public auth recovery visual system is converted to shared tokens, squircle geometry, structural loading/error states, and intentional motion.

### Page 21 Admission - Onboarding

Audit status: intake started 2026-07-06; the Onboarding public-registration visual surface is admitted for guarded continuation on 2026-07-07 after its first full visual pass and a signed-out desktop/mobile-safety rendered proof of the step-1 grid, hover, and expanded organization-type card. Decision: the Onboarding surface at `/onboarding` — the route shell `OnboardingPage.jsx`, the wizard `OnboardingWizard.jsx`, and the five step components `OrganizationTypeStep.jsx`, `AdminAccountStep.jsx`, `OrganizationDetailsStep.jsx`, `InitialSetupStep.jsx`, and `VerificationStep.jsx` — is admitted under the Today/Requests canon as a public registration surface, visual surface only. Only the visual surface is admitted. The registration-flow receivers stay excluded: admin account creation, organization/hospital identity, existing-facility claim, Storage document upload, verification/admin consequence, skip consequence, final submit, and success handoff remain intake-blocked until backend authority proof closes. No registration-flow rewrite, account/org/facility creation promotion, document upload promotion, or Requests multi-data pattern reuse is authorized. The wizard must not be submitted from a proof session because submit performs live account/org/Storage/verification writes.

Why Onboarding is Page 21:

- `/onboarding` is a public route mounted outside `ProtectedRoute`.
- `routes.jsx` names `/onboarding` and `/onboarding-success` as `public: true`, while `ProtectedRoute.jsx` redirects authenticated users with pending onboarding back to `/onboarding`.
- `AppShell` hides the authenticated console shell, shared nav, right context panel, FAB, bottom bar, and startup overlay for `/onboarding`; this is a public registration-shell exception, not a reusable console page layout.
- `OnboardingPage.jsx` keeps completed authenticated users out of the route, but allows pending or skipped onboarding users to continue the registration flow.
- The route crosses multiple authority owners: Supabase Auth sign-up, profile `onboarding_status`, hospital search and claim status, selected facility autofill, hospital/organization creation, profile role and organization linkage, Supabase Storage document upload, display IDs, onboarding success handoff, admin verification, and downstream wallet/organization consequences.

A scoped DOM audit (excluding fixed global overlays) reported zero elements on the onboarding surface computing to `rgb(115, 17, 22)` or `rgb(160, 24, 31)`, zero class-attribute hits for `backdrop-blur`/`shadow-premium`/`shadow-xl`/`shadow-2xl`/`bg-gradient`/`squircle-*`/non-canonical radius/`uppercase`/`tracking-wide*`, and zero horizontal overflow at desktop width; the only page reds were global overlays outside Page 21 scope (the iVisit brand logo pin, the theme-toggle glow, and the PWA install prompt). The step-1 indicator showed a neutral white current-step marker (not red), the organization-type cards rendered as calm `bg-card` tiles with muted icon tiles, and the expanded organization-type card rendered with a neutral white CTA (`rgb(255, 255, 255)`, formerly a red `bg-primary` play button), calm muted feature pills, and a muted icon tile. The standalone public shell (Back to Login header, `&copy;` footer) showed no authenticated console nav, no bottom bar, and no startup overlay, and the browser console reported no errors or warnings. Steps 2-5 were certified statically by the strict-radius hardgate, the residual-token grep, and hand review rather than driven, to avoid the step-3 hospital search read and the final submit writes.

Onboarding intake decisions:

- Admin account receiver: partially proved, not admitted. `createAdminAccount()` delegates to Supabase Auth sign-up and then updates the profile, but the page still needs proof for email-confirmation behavior, trigger timing, existing accounts, rate limits, disabled sign-up, profile update failure, and whether account creation belongs behind a shared auth/onboarding adapter.
- Organization identity: not admitted. The maintained organization/wallet audit says organization wallet initialization follows an `organizations` insert, while current onboarding inserts into `hospitals` and stores that hospital id in `profiles.organization_id`. This cannot yet prove organization identity, wallet initialization, billing scope, or app reconciliation.
- Existing facility claim: not admitted. `OnboardingContext.jsx` stores `selectedHospitalId` and `isClaimingExisting`, but `submitOnboarding()` does not consume either field and always inserts a new `hospitals` row. The duplicate `searchHospitalsByName` definitions also mean the later schema-safe search overrides the earlier pending/claimed status logic.
- Storage documents: not admitted. Document upload writes to the `documents` bucket under `organizations/${organization.id}/verification/${doc.name}`, but bucket policy, file type/size limits, retry behavior, failure surfacing, and verification receiver consequence need proof before upload UI can be promoted.
- Verification/admin consequence: not admitted. The service exposes pending/approve/reject helpers on `hospitals.verification_status`, but active onboarding submit, admin queue, profiles role updates, app-facing provider readiness, and verification status reads need an end-to-end receiver chain before copy can promise approval outcomes.
- Skip onboarding: safer, not admitted. `skipOnboarding()` updates the profile to `viewer` and `skipped`, then navigates to `/` and reloads the app; it now shares the submit lock. That path still needs role access, recovery, dashboard consequence, and no-loop proof before it is treated as a finished public flow.
- Success handoff: not admitted. `/onboarding-success` is a separate public route and will need its own Page 22 intake before the success state can be visually or behaviorally promoted.
- Shell/layout: public-shell exception only. Onboarding may hide the authenticated console frame, but it cannot define a second global shell pattern for console pages.
- Data/privacy copy: not admitted. The active route shell no longer promises emergency response service readiness, but registration copy, verification copy, document upload copy, facility claim copy, and success handoff copy still need receiver-backed review before admission.
- Hardgate: visual surface admitted 2026-07-07. The seven Onboarding visual files (`OnboardingPage.jsx`, `OnboardingWizard.jsx`, `OrganizationTypeStep.jsx`, `AdminAccountStep.jsx`, `OrganizationDetailsStep.jsx`, `InitialSetupStep.jsx`, `VerificationStep.jsx`) are now in the default `scripts/check-ui-surface-hardgate.js` surface. The backend-authority files `OnboardingContext.jsx` and `onboardingService.js` stay out of the hardgate until the registration-flow receivers are proved and admitted. Strict-radius proof for the whole surface may be run with `npm run check:ui-hardgate:revamp -- src/components/pages/OnboardingPage.jsx src/components/onboarding/OnboardingWizard.jsx src/components/onboarding/OrganizationTypeStep.jsx src/components/onboarding/AdminAccountStep.jsx src/components/onboarding/OrganizationDetailsStep.jsx src/components/onboarding/InitialSetupStep.jsx src/components/onboarding/VerificationStep.jsx`.

Required before Page 21 can move past intake:

1. Define one onboarding flow owner for admin account creation, email confirmation, profile initialization, facility search, existing facility claim, organization/facility creation, document upload, verification submit, skipped registration, success redirect, denied/error states, loading states, and recovery states.
2. Prove the canonical organization/facility identity model against Supabase tables, RLS, triggers, wallet initialization, app-facing organization reads, and admin verification reads.
3. Prove whether onboarding creates a new organization, claims an existing hospital/facility, or supports both through distinct receiver commands.
4. Resolve the duplicate `searchHospitalsByName` definitions and prove claim-status semantics without relying on columns that are absent in the current table.
5. Prove Supabase Storage bucket policy, accepted file fields, failure handling, retry behavior, and admin verification receiver consequence.
6. Prove `/login`, `/set-password`, `/onboarding`, and `/onboarding-success` redirect/deep-link behavior together before changing public auth/registration copy or flow order.
7. Keep unproved registration, security, verification, document upload, facility claim, and operational claims out of the active public onboarding surface; any future copy change must stay simple and receiver-backed.
8. Add desktop, tablet, and mobile rendered proof with no authenticated-shell leakage, no startup overlay, no duplicate nav, visible loading/error/empty states, and immediate feedback on every step, submit, skip, toggle, upload, link, and route transition.
9. Add hardgate coverage only after the public onboarding visual system is converted to shared tokens, squircle geometry, structural loading/error states, and intentional motion.

Onboarding Requests-canon blocker map:

This is a blocker map, not a design target. Page 21 may not reuse Requests visual language because Onboarding is a public registration wizard, not a multi-data work stage.

| Requests canon slot | Onboarding current evidence | Page 21 admission decision |
|---|---|---|
| Signal field | Onboarding opens with `Join iVisit` and review setup copy. | Registration-specific. Future signal must say the current step and next action without dashboard metrics or operational claims. |
| State choices | Wizard steps choose type, account, details, setup, and verify. | Do not copy Requests state cards. Preserve one action per step with clear back, skip, save, and retry states. |
| Handled sheet | There is no operational handled sheet. The wizard is the route surface. | Not applicable. Onboarding should stay focused on registration progress and receiver proof. |
| Focused detail | Hospital search, selected facility autofill, documents, and profile details are the main detail states. | Intake only. Must prove claim/update/create semantics before visual promotion. |
| Route-owned action | Continue, create account, submit, skip, upload, Back to Login, and success redirect exist. Final submit is awaited and account/submit/skip share a submit lock. | Safer, not admitted. Future pass must prove pressed, pending, success, failure, denied, disabled, retry, and redirect feedback. |
| Shared modal/sheet | Onboarding uses no console `ModalShell`/`FilterSheet`. | Public onboarding exception. Do not introduce private console modal patterns from Onboarding into authenticated pages. |
| Mobile recomposition | The wizard route/shell source now uses shared squircle radius tokens, but no rendered Page 21 proof exists yet and step form chrome is not certified. | Not admitted. Must prove mobile keyboard, safe-area, upload, footer, and step navigation behavior before visual work. |
| Data quieting | AuthProvider wraps the route, but `AppShell` disables console chrome and startup overlay for `/onboarding`. | Partially proved. Future pass must prove no protected domain startup reads are caused by Onboarding. |
| Local semantic color | Current UI uses primary/destructive/muted feedback across wizard, validation, and claim states. | Intake only. Future color must separate available claim, pending verification, verified block, receiver failure, upload failure, and success. |
| Interaction feedback | Loading, toasts, disabled buttons, step transitions, draft persistence, awaited submit, and guarded skip/submit paths exist. | Intake only. Future proof must cover every click/tap/submit/upload, including receiver errors and route transitions. |

Promotion rule: the first Onboarding visual pass on 2026-07-07 admitted the visual surface only and added the seven Onboarding visual files to the default hardgate; the blocker map above still gates any registration-flow receiver promotion. `OnboardingContext.jsx` and `onboardingService.js` remain out of the default hardgate as backend-authority files, and Onboarding may not reuse Requests multi-data visual language until its receiver, identity, storage, verification, and app-consequence blockers close.

### Page 22 Admission - Onboarding Success

Audit status: intake started 2026-07-06; the Onboarding Success visual surface is admitted for guarded continuation on 2026-07-07 after its first visual pass and desktop/mobile-safety rendered proof of both the submitted and missing states. Decision: `OnboardingSuccessPage` is admitted under the Today/Requests canon as a public confirmation-recovery surface only. Only the visual surface is admitted; the success-state source owner, display-ID identity truth (Page 21 organization-vs-hospital), verification/review timing, dashboard-access eligibility, and support-receiver remain excluded until backend/Page-21 proof. No success-flow rewrite, dashboard-access promise, review-timing promise, or display-ID product promotion is admitted.

Why Onboarding Success is Page 22:

- `/onboarding-success` is a public route mounted outside `ProtectedRoute`.
- `routes.jsx` names `/onboarding-success` as `public: true`, and `ProtectedRoute.jsx` includes it in the public allowed path list.
- `AppShell` hides the authenticated console shell, shared nav, right context panel, FAB, bottom bar, and startup overlay for `/onboarding-success`; this is a public confirmation-shell exception, not a reusable console page layout.
- `OnboardingContext.jsx` navigates to `/onboarding-success` with `location.state.result` only after `onboardingService.submitOnboarding(formData)` returns success.
- The route crosses multiple authority owners: Onboarding submit result state, display ID evidence, profile role/onboarding status, hospital/organization identity, verification review status, dashboard route access, support handoff, and direct browser navigation behavior when `location.state` is missing.

HEAD snapshot evidence for this ledger: `git show HEAD:frontend/src/components/pages/OnboardingSuccessPage.jsx`, `git show HEAD:frontend/src/contexts/OnboardingContext.jsx`, `git show HEAD:frontend/src/services/onboardingService.js`, `git show HEAD:frontend/src/App.js`, `git show HEAD:frontend/src/config/routes.jsx`, and `git show HEAD:frontend/src/components/common/ProtectedRoute.jsx` showed the old/current behavior inventory: success route mounted without `ProtectedRoute`, shell-hidden public route handling, `useLocation()` state read, optional organization/user display ID cards, `Registration Submitted!`, `Your organization registration is being reviewed.`, `Organization Identity`, `Administrator Identity`, `What happens next?`, `Verification in 24-48 hours`, `Start using iVisit`, `Go to Dashboard`, `mailto:support@ivisit.ng`, and no route-owned data refetch when `location.state` is missing.

- Targeted recent history check: `git log --since='2026-06-28' -- frontend/src/components/pages/OnboardingSuccessPage.jsx frontend/src/App.js frontend/src/config/routes.jsx frontend/src/components/common/ProtectedRoute.jsx frontend/src/contexts/OnboardingContext.jsx frontend/src/services/onboardingService.js` returned no newer committed changes for those anchor files. The Onboarding Success preservation baseline for this intake pass remains committed `HEAD` at `f31f29f`; active source is uncommitted source on top of that baseline.
- Active source recertification: `OnboardingSuccessPage.jsx` still owns the visible public success route, reads `location.state?.result`, displays organization/user display IDs only when present on a successful submitted path, renders the review and dashboard-access copy only when `result.success` is present, links to `/` only on the submitted path, renders a missing-state fallback when navigation state is absent, and uses a `mailto:` support handoff. `OnboardingContext.jsx` still reaches this page only after `submitOnboarding()` success. `onboardingService.js` still returns the inserted organization row plus display IDs after profile update and document upload attempts.
- First missing-state safety cleanup on 2026-07-06 kept the normal submitted-registration view behind `result.success`, but direct visits, refreshes, and missing navigation state now render `Registration status unavailable` with a return-to-registration action instead of displaying success copy without submitted evidence.
- Onboarding Success public-confirmation squircle/source-feedback cleanup on 2026-07-06 converted `OnboardingSuccessPage.jsx` local confirmation chrome to semantic radius tokens (`rounded-icon`, `rounded-inner`, `rounded-pill`), removed decorative radius/tracking/all-caps chrome, removed literal green/orange success/identity tones, and replaced unproved review timing/dashboard-access copy (`Verification in 24-48 hours`, `Start using iVisit`, and `Go to Dashboard`) with simple review-handoff copy and `Open console`. This is focused strict-radius/source-voice proof only; it does not admit Onboarding Success, prove the success-state source, prove verification timing, prove dashboard eligibility, prove support routing, prove rendered mobile confirmation proof, or add Onboarding Success to the default hardgate.
- First Onboarding Success visual pass on 2026-07-07 admitted the `OnboardingSuccessPage` visual surface. Radius was already canonical from the prior cleanup; this pass removed the remaining decorative chrome from the display-ID cards and dots (`backdrop-blur-xl`, `bg-gradient-to-br` orbs, `shadow-premium`, the looping `pulse-dot`, and `bg-white/5`/`bg-white/10` glass) and moved the surface off heavy brand-red to calm neutral tones: a muted `CheckCircle`/`AlertCircle` tile (`bg-muted`/`text-foreground`/`text-muted-foreground`), muted display-ID icon tiles with subtle neutral `rounded-pill` markers, neutral `Clock`/`Shield` icons, and neutral `bg-foreground`/`text-background` primary actions (`Open console` / `Continue registration`). It preserved both branches, `location.state?.result` reads, the `hasSubmissionResult` gate, the display-ID cards, the honest review/console copy, and the `mailto:` support handoff. `OnboardingSuccessPage.jsx` is now in the default UI hardgate and passes `npm run check:ui-hardgate:revamp -- src/components/pages/OnboardingSuccessPage.jsx`.
- Onboarding Success preservation ledger: the old `Registration Submitted!` / `Your organization registration is being reviewed.` / `Organization Identity` / `Administrator Identity` copy and the unproved `Verification in 24-48 hours` / `Start using iVisit` / `Go to Dashboard` SLA/dashboard promises were already converted to honest copy before this pass and stay converted. The decorative glass/blur/glow/pulse chrome and brand-red decoration are converted this pass. The `location.state` reads, both branches, display-ID cards, and support handoff are preserved. The success-state source owner, display-ID identity truth, verification timing, dashboard-access eligibility, and support receiver remain excluded until backend/Page-21 proof.
